# Coverage: channels

Operations: 33/36
Conditions: 468/907 (465 ambient, 442 event)
Entry points: 313/438 (125 excluded)
Breaks mapped: 3/6

Counting rule: these counts describe the written plan after the declared helpers and persona additions exist; no phase has been executed as this template. An operation is covered when a phase performs it without a real platform account. An event condition is covered when its event flow can perform it without a real platform account. An ambient condition is covered by the persona content or the setup that supplies it. Entry points count as placed when assigned to one operation, even when a live adapter remains outside reach. Incomplete flows and account-dependent flows remain listed below; a combined condition is not counted as fully planned while one of its variants remains outside reach.

Uncovered:
- 96. Native replies to the bot count as mentions on Discord Microsoft Teams QQBot Slack and Telegram (condition): outside planned reach. Current group reply input uses the conformance adapter with a bot-last reply label. No supplied native gated-group reply to the actual bot establishes the listed platform producer. Telegram direct-chat reply/media cases do not exercise group mention admission.
- 97. Native quotes of the bot count as mentions on LINE WhatsApp and Zalo personal (condition): partial. The WhatsApp quoted-bot recipe supplies a genuine native quote but requires a real account. The conformance bot-last-quote label does not supply the LINE WhatsApp or Zalo native quoted-bot fact; no account-free producer completes this condition.

The Slack joined-thread recipe supplies an actual native participation case only with a real account. No supplied native LINE Mattermost Slack or Tlon disabled-fact observation exists.

- 672. Late failure or suppression cannot revoke an accepted final (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied case first confirms final acceptance and then records late failure and suppression on that same native preview owner. An initial failed final or a cleanup failure does not establish this ordered transition.
- 673. Suppression cannot mask an existing final-delivery failure (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied case records native final failure before an intentional suppression on the same owner and observes the retained failure. This differs from suppression after acceptance and from an initially suppressed payload.
- 674. Retaining a preview transfers cleanup custody without claiming final delivery (condition): outside planned reach. Existing error-final retention already has accepted delivery, and ambiguous-edit retention follows edit uncertainty. Neither explicitly calls the real retainPreview custody transition without final acceptance and then observes cleanup and final-delivery projections.
- 675. A legacy finalizer callback resolving void acknowledges delivery (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied phase selects the published legacy finalizer contract with an actual void-returning delivery callback and records acceptance. Stateful explicit result objects and ordinary successful sends do not establish this compatibility branch.
- 676. Beginning finalization stops progress synchronously while delivery remains pending (condition): outside planned reach. Delayed-finalization captures a pending transport but does not explicitly observe onFinalStarted ordering, pending final state and continued native transport custody at the actual beginFinalDelivery boundary. Beginning is not acceptance or transport cancellation.
- 677. Failed or suppressed final sends do not trigger successful-final cleanup (condition): outside planned reach. Existing unused-preview and accepted-error recipes do not supply a failed or suppressed replacement final with the same preview and observe cleanup eligibility. Later explicit cleanupUndelivered behavior remains distinct from automatic successful-final cleanup.
- 678. Non-final progress delivery does not establish final acceptance (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied observation captures native final-delivery projections after an accepted tool/block delivery but before any final delivery. Visible progress and a progress receipt alone are not final evidence.
- 679. Inspection returning no event ignores input before durable append (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied monitor case returns null inspection facts and records ignored admission with no durable append or dispatch. SDK sender denial and claimed identity mismatch are different paths.
- 680. Unsupported stored ingress payload version is rejected before delivery (condition): outside planned reach. The existing decoded ID/lane mismatch condition 566 does not name or supply an invalid-version envelope. No supplied native fixture exercises version rejection before deserialization/delivery.
- 681. Append backoff preserves the order of accepted ingress admissions (condition): outside planned reach. Existing exhaustion records append failure, while condition 649 covers a pending asynchronous inspector. Neither supplied sequence captures a retried first durable append and a later accepted admission to establish the backoff ordering boundary.
- 682. An adopted ingress claim can still own a pending delivery that must be joined (condition): outside planned reach. Existing shutdown cases retain unadopted work. No supplied observation first terminally adopts the claim, leaves its delivery promise active, and observes activity/idle or stop waiting for that separate delivery lifetime.
- 683. Repeated queue availability checks open one queue before drain startup (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied case calls the actual idempotent queue availability operation repeatedly before start, observes one queue opening and performs the supported preparation interval before any drain begins. Merely starting an account does not establish this transition.
- 684. Stopping during pending preparation closes the lazy drain creation race (condition): outside planned reach. The existing shutdown and asynchronous claim-inspection cases operate with an established drain. No supplied case holds preparation before lazy drain creation while stop wins, then observes the released continuation and settled disposal.
- 685. Empty or undefined-only claim fan-in yields no combined lifecycle (condition): outside planned reach. Existing batch observations pass real nonempty claims. None records the actual empty/undefined-only fan-in branch and its no-lifecycle result; ordinary single-event dispatch does not invoke this branch.
- 686. Deferred heartbeat renewal stops with native lifecycle settlement or ownership loss (condition): outside planned reach. The no-heartbeat watchdog case covers missing renewal. Existing batch contracts preserve callbacks but do not supply and capture positive periodic renewal through adoption/completion, ownership abort and heartbeat-callback failure, without separate keepalive timers.
- 687. Claim fan-in chooses the shortest positive finite deferred heartbeat cadence (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied batch gives source lifecycles different positive cadences plus invalid/absent cadences and observes the actual aggregate cadence. Several claims alone do not establish cadence selection.
- 688. Reserved or refreshed outbound source identity suppresses a racing self echo (condition): outside planned reach. The existing echo case follows an already recorded outbound message. No supplied observation races an echo before send-response identity capture or after removal of the native bound route while its source identity is reserved/refreshed.
- 689. Plain-text sanitization uses Markdown markers when the channel reparses Markdown (condition): outside planned reach. The existing corrected sanitizer case selects default chat-style markers. No supplied account or input selects the documented Markdown style and records its distinct bold/strikethrough markers. This is not another Markdown table-mode condition.
- 690. Destination-only receipt metadata does not establish a platform message identity (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied result contains only destination/routing identifiers with no message/poll/receipt identity and captures the receipt classification. An empty generic result or a receipt labelled suppressed is not this metadata-shape distinction.
- 691. A single provider-observed receipt thread overrides the requested thread (condition): outside planned reach. The corrected conflicting-thread case supplies two provider threads. It does not provide the separate one-provider-thread versus different requested-thread case that should retain a single aggregate thread.
- 692. Send-success and commit callbacks run at different native delivery boundaries (condition): outside planned reach. The ordinary conformance record contains both callback names, but no supplied observation correlates each with provider acceptance and native queue acknowledgment. Callback presence alone does not demonstrate their different lifecycle boundaries or preservation when using a derived adapter.
- 693. A receipt thread is not adopted without the adapter opt-in (condition): outside planned reach. The existing first-send thread case deliberately uses an opting-in Discord adapter. No supplied case returns a provider thread without adoptTargetFromDelivery and observes later payload routing under that omission.
- 694. Automatic unknown-send reconciliation selects exactly one post-policy payload (condition): outside planned reach. Existing reconciliation condition 589 describes eventual reconciliation outcomes. No supplied observation varies cancellation/preparation from zero to one to several accepted payloads while retaining the actual automatic opt-in decision.
- 695. Required reconciliation rejects an unsupported prepared shape or transport kind before provider I/O (condition): outside planned reach. Original fail-closed proof describes recovery without authoritative proof. No supplied case requests reconciliation for an unsupported prepared shape or a selected branch excluded by reconcileUnknownSendKinds and records the pre-I/O rejection.
- 696. Unknown-send recovery reuses prepared provider payloads without rerunning modifiers (condition): outside planned reach. The original persisted-state outcome composite does not describe modifier invocation counts or payload-regeneration avoidance. No supplied observation captures the prepared provider plan across recovery together with no repeated modifying hook or regeneration.
- 697. An explicit allowed deferred-delivery admission decision permits the send (condition): outside planned reach. Ordinary conformance sends already use the absent-hook default. No supplied case installs the explicit allowed admission decision and captures its live and recovery continuation. Condition 590 records the distinct permanent-rejection alternatives; omission is not claimed to be wholly unobserved.
- 576. Preview retained after accepted error final, or unused with no final or failure and cleanup only when configured (condition): partial. The accepted-error and silent-after-preview inputs remain. The declared owner options do not supply or capture both cleanupUndelivered false and true for an unused preview with no final or failure. The conditional cleanup meaning was explicitly recorded in the second run’s condition 455 and remains part of this existing condition.
- 579. HTML formatting sanitized for plain-text transport while comparison prose remains literal (condition): partial. The real sanitizer producer sends bold and strikethrough HTML only. It supplies no documented comparison-prose input such as an unspaced angle comparison, so literal preservation remains outside planned reach. the second run’s condition 458 already recorded this meaning; it receives no new ID or discovery credit.
- 588. First-send thread adopted for later payloads and pins while explicit caller threads are preserved (condition): partial. The real forum batch explicitly requires the absence of a caller thread and sends two text payloads. It has no explicit-thread preservation counterpart or pin carrying the adopted thread. Those alternatives were already recorded in the second run’s condition 465 and remain part of this existing condition.
- 590. Deferred delivery permanently rejected before live queueing or marked failed during recovery (condition): partial. The existing phase sends once to the noDeferredAccount and records live output/state. It supplies no previously queued intent reaching the native recovery admission hook, failed settlement and skipped reconciliation/replay. Live and recovery rejection were both explicitly recorded in both runs in both recorded rounds.
- 662. New Telegram text and rich-text sends use fresh built-in HTTP connections rather than idle pooled sockets (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied observation captures native connection identity or reuse for sendMessage and sendRichMessage through the built-in transport. Existing new-message, preview, reply, error and retry observations record application requests/results, not whether an idle socket was borrowed or a fresh connection opened.
- 663. Telegram polling, edits and control requests retain built-in HTTP connection pooling (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied observation establishes the built-in pooled connection behavior for polling, edits and control requests. Existing getUpdates, preview-edit and control calls do not capture connection reuse; a successful request or retained message identity does not establish the transport pool used.
- 664. Ordinary ingress delivery returning without an explicit handoff terminally adopts the no-dispatch event (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied case returns normally from the native monitor delivery callback without adopting, deferring, failing, cancelling or abandoning, then captures the resulting native adoption. Existing cases specify real delivery, explicit lifecycle callbacks or cancellation; generic policy denial is not this post-claim no-handoff return.
- 665. Preview reset while an earlier completion is awaited fences that completion from the new generation (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied case captures an awaited operation of one native preview owner, resets that same owner for the next admitted generation with the corresponding transport message identity rotation, then releases and observes the earlier completion. Ordinary next replies, preview cancellation, cleanup failure and session reset do not establish this owner-generation boundary.
- 657. An advertised provider prefix selects the outbound channel when the requested channel is last or omitted (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied command requests last or omits selection in the native session-delivery path while supplying a recognized provider-prefixed target. Explicit bare targets and the exactly-one-configured-channel default are different branches.
- 658. A provider prefix matching an explicitly selected outbound channel preserves that selection (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied command combines an explicitly selected channel with its advertised provider prefix. The existing Discord channel: targets use a target-kind prefix, not the advertised discord: provider prefix.
- 659. A provider prefix belonging to a different explicitly selected outbound channel is rejected before adapter target normalization (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied command combines an explicit selected channel with a different runtime-visible plugin's advertised provider prefix. Native account selection and cross-conversation authorization observations do not reach this target-prefix rejection.
- 660. A prefix not advertised by a runtime-visible channel plugin does not select an outbound provider (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied command attempts channel inference from a non-advertised prefix with the native selection context captured. A plugin name or alias alone is insufficient: the prefix must occur in its messaging.targetPrefixes declaration.
- 661. Target-kind and service prefixes stay within the selected channel grammar and do not select an outbound provider (condition): partial. Existing explicit Discord channel: targets supply only part of the selected-adapter grammar. No supplied complete sequence covers the documented target-kind/service alternatives and demonstrates that those prefixes alone do not choose a provider. Unspecified persona target strings are not evidence of their content.
- 525. Channel ingress result presented after context or plugin/Gateway ownership changes does not gain host authority (condition): partial. event-ingress-scope-changed keeps one live plugin owner and presents its exact resolver result to a different conversation context. No supplied observation retains and redeems that result across actual plugin retirement/replacement or another Gateway. Those recorded ownership alternatives are outside planned reach; ordinary plugin reload, process restart and stale queued-participant observations do not establish the resolver-to-context handoff.
- 656. An already consumed channel ingress resolver result reused in another context handoff does not gain host authority (condition): outside planned reach. No supplied observation first consumes the exact host-issued result in a valid context and then presents that same object again with otherwise unchanged owner and scope. Replaying a platform message, creating a fresh resolver result, duplicating stored text or changing context scope does not exercise this one-shot guard. Native private evidence is not copied or modified to manufacture the case.

- 388. Turn ended or replaced while narration request pending, cancelled (condition): partial. The existing native Discord case captures a pending utility request and ends its original turn. No separate native replacement-turn producer is supplied while that request remains pending, so the replacement alternative is outside planned reach.

- 555. Queued turn mixing several participants or stale evidence, attribution unknown (condition): outside the current planned reach. No supported producer in the supplied SDK fixture yields two distinct admitted participants in one compatible native collect group. The running predecessor is not a queued contribution. Distinct ordinary sender inputs produce different native authorization keys and are split; changing sender authority, admission evidence or lifecycle identity to force collection would manufacture the state. No separate stale-evidence producer is supplied.

- 560. Reaction, button, callback or native command from unknown sender cannot start pairing (condition): partial. The captured-control callback and reaction inputs exercise their native paths with unknown senders. No corresponding native-command input from an unknown sender is supplied; that distinct admission mode remains outside planned reach.
- 594. Question settlement edits Telegram, Discord and Slack prompts; Mattermost retains externally settled prompts and LINE later taps receive a notice (condition): partial. The declared Telegram cases supply answering and expiry. Deliberate cancellation elsewhere, the corresponding Discord/Slack prompt edits, Mattermost prompt retention after external settlement and a later LINE tap notice are not supplied. Original the second final run’s condition 471 already named the Mattermost and LINE alternatives, so restoring them adds no condition or discovery credit. Mattermost private feedback and accepted-click retirement are separately inventoried as 705–706.

- 122. Identical repeated group batch skipped as echo (condition): outside the current planned reach. The source document describes remembering the combined history and current-message prompt. The removed event-echo-and-repeated-batch input created two fresh events and captured no native batch replay. The pinned shared and WhatsApp paths inspected here guard message/source identity; no supported producer for the distinct documented combined-prompt echo boundary was established. Ordinary message-ID replay, native debounce batching and a captured outbound-message echo remain separate observations and do not supply this condition.

- 33. Slack or Discord thread keyed as its own session (condition): partially planned. A conformance or Discord thread does not establish the named Slack alternative.
- 75. Clean empty or reasoning-only group turn treated as NO_REPLY (condition): partially planned. event-silent-empty-turn supplies one empty-turn input. The persona calls its single marker an empty or reasoning-only script, but no separate controlled reasoning-only invocation or two-result recipe is declared. The empty-turn observation is retained; it does not establish both output forms.
- 92. Discord and Slack group DMs controlled separately (condition): partially planned. The Discord persona/input is retained, while the native Slack MPIM/control observations in live-slack-settings require a real Slack workspace. The combined row cannot receive full account-free credit from the Discord side or from a shared persona description alone.
- 304. Inbound dedupe cache entry expired or evicted (condition): partially planned. event-dedupe-cache-expired-prepare and its replay explicitly insert5001 newer IDs after a fresh captured input, supplying capacity eviction. No flow retains that entry through the unchanged native twenty-minute TTL before exact replay without eviction; the time-expiry alternative is unplanned.
- 421. Discord send or REST call 429, timeout or 5xx retried (condition): partially planned. event-discord-send-retried and its send-retry helper explicitly supply429,HTTP502 and a connection reset. They declare no actual request-timeout interval. A reset is a separate transport failure and does not establish the named timeout alternative.
- 615. Acknowledgment reactions disabled (ackReaction empty or scope off) (condition): partially planned. message-agent-typing-and-acks-1 applies ackReactionScope off while explicitly setting a nonempty acknowledgement emoji. No retained command or persona variant supplies an explicit empty ackReaction under an enabled scope, so only the scope-off alternative is planned.
- 573. Claim taken by another owner during fan-in adoption fails the rest (condition): outside reach. No supplied fixture performs a genuine native ownership transition for the captured live claim. A callback exception or direct database identity rewrite does not establish another native owner; successful multiple-claim settlement remains planned under 572.
- 589. Ambiguous provider send reconciled as sent, not_sent or unresolved with fail-closed proof and best-effort failed-row cleanup (condition): outside reach. Telegram has no automatic reconciliation opt-in/callback, and the current conformance adapter has no authoritative persisted provider proof or reconciliation callback. Lost-response injection plus restart cannot supply these capabilities. Matrix has the native capability but needs a real account and a supplied correlated provider fixture. Its recorded sent/not_sent/unresolved, fail-closed and best-effort terminal-cleanup alternatives remain under this same ID.
- 377. Failed or blocked draft write leaves previous plan (condition): outside reach. The native progress_card tool calls in-process progressCard.put and emits a replacement plan only after a successful result. A Telegram editMessageText fault cannot induce that write failure. No supported owner-scoped public or OS failure fixture is supplied; the existing recipe retains successful create/clear coverage for 376 only.
- 427. Durable delivery producer claim expired or replaced, budget not charged (condition): partially covered by event-send-started-lease-expired for expiry only. Replaced-owner reservation remains outside reach: the declared fixture has no independent native owner that can replace this exact queue claim while the stale caller remains live. No replacement flow or synthetic claim mutation is credited.
- 626. Crash after detached completion entered a parent turn with a channel route (condition): requires a real Codex harness and captured parent-completion admission; generic child spawn acceptance is insufficient; written, outside reach
- 647. An older host ignores the asynchronous companion and uses the synchronous fallback (condition): no older host is supplied at the pinned current revision; written, outside reach
- 264. After login, an account start skipped or owned elsewhere reports its reason while saved auth remains (condition): successful auth.login needs a real WhatsApp account and a controlled pending start; written, outside reach
- Link a channel account by QR or interactive login (operation): completing the login needs a real QR-capable account; the no-channel error probe does not cover completion
- Set the bot's profile and presence (operation): outside planned reach. The retained fixture-only Discord presence input does not cover the combined profile operation. All Nostr profile UI controls and all three profile HTTP entries remain placed here without an executable profile flow. The former UI recipe is removed because Save publishes signed replaceable profile events to the same supplied identity's relays across comparison clones, and Import reads that shared latest profile and writes merged local configuration. Neither remote baseline isolation nor exclusive baseline-restoring ownership is supplied; a real account alone is insufficient. GET profile also has no dedicated executable observation. Matrix set-profile remains placed here without a mutation command for the same missing remote-identity isolation. No per-clone credentials, relay service or coordination mechanism is implied.

Nostr profile selection limit: the dedicated profile controls and GET/PUT/POST observations remain unavailable. Their owners are extensions/nostr/src/nostr-profile-http.ts, nostr-profile.ts, nostr-profile-import.ts and the profile-publication branch of nostr-bus.ts, plus ui/src/pages/channels/view.nostr-profile-form.ts, nostr-profile-ops.ts and the Nostr handlers in channels-page.ts. Shared-file or broad adapter selectors may still select ordinary incoming-message or Channels-page observations; those observations do not exercise profile publication/import. Removing the profile-only selectors from the remaining Discord presence phase loses none of the 99 placed callable source-file selections. The only member of that set affected by the removed patterns, ui/src/lib/channels/index.ts, still selects existing status/setup/config/pairing/QR/logout observations and their captured cleanup.

The rename, icon, participant and leave recipes are withdrawn because they mutate the same supplied remote Messages group or membership across comparison clones. All five action entries remain placed in this operation without an executable recipe.

- [OpenClaw #165600](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165600) (break): chat.send on an Incognito Control UI session, owned by sessions; outside this template
- [OpenClaw #166049](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/166049) (break): the Control UI public-thread chat link, owned by sessions; the X adapter paths need a real X account; outside this template

- 202. WhatsApp disconnect and relogin loop flagged as recent reconnects (condition): its WhatsApp reconnect phases require a real account; written, outside reach
- 203. WhatsApp status=408 request time-out loop (condition): its WhatsApp timeout-loop phases require a real account; written, outside reach
- 471. WhatsApp starts automatically when a linked session exists (condition): its WhatsApp auto-start phase requires a linked real account; written, outside reach
- 484. iMessage inbound recovery after bridge or Gateway restart (condition): its iMessage bridge and Gateway recovery phases require a macOS Messages host; written, outside reach
- 523. QR login start and wait with omitted channel select the first loaded QR-capable provider (condition): selecting a loaded QR-capable provider requires the real QR account configuration; written, outside reach

- 548. Inbound metadata write failure reports the record error while the turn continues (condition): outside reach. No supplied supported fault reaches the native session metadata writer independently of ordinary turn persistence. A synthetic callback error or generic table-write toggle does not prove this path.

- 565. Durable ingress append retries exhausted, transport callback rejected (condition): outside reach. No supplied owner-scoped public or OS fixture causes the native durable ingress queue enqueue writes to fail through their real append retry budget. Do not replace the native queue or mutate its rows.

- 583. Required durable send intent persistence failing stops delivery (condition): outside reach. No supplied owner-scoped public or OS fixture fails native required outbound intent persistence before dispatch. A fictional per-table fault callback is not this producer.

- 584. Best-effort durable send queue write fails, live-only send (condition): outside reach. No supplied owner-scoped persistence fault and actual best_effort producer reaches native live-only fallback. Changing a fault name does not establish queue-write failure or best_effort policy.

- 613. Gateway timeout after accepted agent turn with --deliver, may still deliver (condition): outside reach. No supplied independent native CLI transport-loss/wait fixture exercises the accepted agent --deliver ambiguity path while preserving the intended run. Killing the CLI bypasses that path; pausing the Gateway past timeout+30 also expires its own timeout budget.

- 623. Message-tool-only turn without durable ingress claim at recovery tombstoned (condition): outside reach. No supplied native producer creates a message-tool-only interrupted turn without reconstructable durable ingress authority. Dropping or editing its claim would manufacture the security condition.

- 414. Spoiler markers rendered on Signal and Telegram, plain elsewhere (condition): partial. Telegram, Discord and conformance outputs are planned, but no phase supplies the Signal rendering variant. Signal requires a real account and its native transport; the combined condition is excluded from fully planned coverage.

- 301. Pairing RPC to an older Gateway, missing credentials, refusal or uncertain reply never falls back locally and does not bootstrap or notify after an unacknowledged approval (condition): partial. The retained owner-refusal flow records OWNER_REFUSED. No supplied sequence establishes OUTCOME_UNKNOWN after actual dispatch; refusal is not proof of uncertain acceptance or its no-replay behavior. Older-Gateway and missing-credential variants are not supplied by that refusal flow either. The combined condition is excluded from fully planned coverage.

- 400. Heartbeat target none, unresolved, delivery-disabled or non-typing shows no typing (condition): partial. The existing target:none input is retained, but no supplied flow establishes an unresolved route, disabled chat delivery or a channel without typing capability under otherwise valid heartbeat conditions. These distinct native guards are not proved by the ordinary typing-capable target. The combined condition is excluded from fully planned coverage.

- 382. Custom, pooled or hidden progress label (condition): partially planned. The retained configured observations set the explicit custom label Kova. No separate adopted label:false configuration or nonempty labels pool and matching route is supplied, so hidden and pooled labels remain unplanned. The combined condition is excluded from fully planned coverage.

- 22. Several accounts on one channel without a default, first normalized id used (condition): outside reach. The former input explicitly supplied an account and shared the configured-default environment, so it could not observe default selection. That input and its contradictory same-environment trait requirement are removed. No supplied flow establishes the complete account list with both configured preference and a default-named account absent, then invokes native selection without an override.
- 466. Heartbeat showOk true delivers HEARTBEAT_OK (condition): outside reach. No supplied effective heartbeatVisibility.showOk:true configuration and matching HEARTBEAT_OK observation exists; an ordinary heartbeat wake retains the opposite native default.
- 467. Heartbeat showAlerts false (condition): outside reach. No supplied effective heartbeatVisibility.showAlerts:false configuration and corresponding alert-suppression observation exists. Timing, target and typing settings do not establish this visibility branch.
- 468. Heartbeat useIndicator false (condition): outside reach. No supplied effective heartbeatVisibility.useIndicator:false configuration and corresponding indicator observation exists. The native default remains true and is not the named case.

- 9. Re-invite after join claim expired introduces again (condition): outside reach. Native join-introduction claims expire after ninety days. No supplied genuinely aged claim with its actual account/room identity and provenance exists, and a fresh join cannot expire within the removed 150-second recipe. No helper edits claim timestamps, shortens the native TTL or changes the clock to manufacture the condition. Ordinary join and duplicate-introduction observations remain.

Persona additions:
- channel-guarded-record-target: An exact native binding for channel-probe-conversations.guardedPeer on the default conformance account with match.peer.kind group and session.groupScope per-group. The group peer is reserved for this recording-only case; its resolved metadata and last-route session targets are absent. Missing/changed binding, main-session routing or an existing target makes observation unavailable; no session is deleted to establish the precondition. (no keys)
- channel-ingress-pre-adoption-gates: Scoped, abort-aware conformance native monitor delivery gates and Telegram shim getFile response gates. Dedicated claimedProbeEventId uses the former; claimedTelegramEventId is a naturally created failed native media update whose retained payload identifies a fixture-owned file for the latter. Neither uses a held model response or rewritten queue payload. (no keys)
- channel-ingress-legacy-host: An already provisioned older OpenClaw host whose documented SDK ignores inspectAsync. Unavailable at the pinned current revision; no runtime installation, synthetic host capability or version override is proposed. (no keys)
- channel-ingress-monitor-fixture: A harness-owned conformance plugin using the installed native durable ingress monitor and account queue, with scoped asynchronous inspection and pre-adoption delivery gates, stable event/lane identities, abort-aware cleanup, and ordered callback/activity observations. It supplies ordinary runtime configuration and supported channel callback options; it does not replace native queues, clocks, claims, watchdogs or retry policy. (no keys)
The input persona also supplies agents-three-configured (three agent identifiers), config-heartbeat-short (heartbeat timing), cron-job-isolated (jobId), gateway-multi-user-profiles (memberProfileId), and mock-provider-held-turn (marker). These are reused content; they require no new trait definition here. legacyPluginCredential is a credential-store name, never a credential value; the unavailable channel-account-add wrapper resolves it without recording the value. The Telegram shim token keys identify synthetic fixture values only.
Every listed key identifies a value containing only letters, digits and . _ - : / @. Definitions name content and identifiers, never secret values; real-account content remains outside reach.
- channel-access-groups: accessGroups with an operators group referenced from DM and group allowlists, a reference to a missing group, an unreferenced group, a sender listed only for another channel, and an account with dmPolicy open without "*" (crossChannelSender, memberSender, missingGroupSender, openNoWildcardAccount, unreferencedSender)
- channel-accounts-multiple: a channel with a default and a named account, plus disabled, autostart-suppressed, secret-missing, unlisted, owner-unresolved and no-deferred-delivery accounts (disabledAccount, namedAccount, noDeferredAccount, ownerlessAccount, secretMissingAccount, suppressedAccount, unlistedAccount)
- channel-ambient-room: rooms with unmentionedInbound room_event and message-tool replies: an always-on room, a still mention-gated room, a room whose agent lacks the message tool, an ackReactionScope all room and a room with an agent-specific override (ackAllRoom, ambientRoom, gatedAmbientRoom, mutedAgentRoom, overrideRoom)
- channel-ambient-turn: a separate conformance account/peer route to a dedicated agent main session, distinct from every ordinary scenario session, with independent mock-provider capacity so a held response does not block the other operations (accountId, peer)
- channel-ambient-unsupported: room_event set on a channel without ambient room event support, which keeps its existing group behaviour (groupId, senderPeer)
- channel-bot-identity-unknown: a channel without a reliable inbound bot identity, keeping its self-message and access filters (botPeer, groupId)
- channel-bot-peer: bot-authored inbound messages between two bots (bot loop protection); and a second bot identity and rooms that accept bots, require a bot mention, refuse bots, disable the loop guard and override it (acceptRoom, botId, channelId, discordChannelId, mentionOnlyRoom, noBotsRoom, overrideRoom, unguardedRoom)
- channel-bot-thread: unmentioned follow-ups accepted in a confirmed bot-created thread (requireMentionInBotThreads false) (evictedThreadId, threadId)
- channel-broadcast-group: a qualified broadcast room on the conformance channel with two participant agents (handles and mention patterns), a spare agent listed in it, and mock markers for a follow-up round, an all-pass round and one failing participant; and broadcast group threads with three agents and distinct mention patterns: a two-round group, a sequential group, a small-budget group, a no-selection group, a route-bound peer, a failing participant and a peer with a configured ACP binding (acpBoundGroup, agentA, agentB, agentC, budgetGroup, failingAgentGroup, groupId, noSelectionGroup, passMarker, roundsGroup, senderPeer, sequentialGroup)
- channel-cli-wizard-answers: The existing configureAnswers and telegramAnswers are scoped native answer scripts for a pre-existing owned Telegram fixture account. New configScopeFile is a nonsecret descriptor naming that account, expected current channel/account selections, exact allowed source paths and a distinct display-name edit value. All other supplied accounts remain selected/enabled; scripts reject unexpected destructive/install/auth prompts. Each configuration or setup invocation records changed-state output then restores its own starting state. The two dedicated channels remove answer files retain their intentional disabled/deleted result and do not use restore-config.
- channel-config-variants: mentionPatch is a nonempty schema-valid scoped patch of the selected conformance group mention/allowlist fields; messagesPatch is a nonempty patch of messages.groupChat, with the controlled group/account and effective per-agent overrides recorded so the native runtime use is observable. The existing configured owner captures/restores original values and absence; files do not set global baseline state for the rest of the run. singleAccountPatch remains under its separately assigned promotion owner. singleAccountPatch is a nonsecret Telegram-only root single-account input with accounts absent, owned exclusively by channel-account-add for its complete promotion and exact restoration sequence.
- channel-debounce: an already supplied conformance receive fixture owning the installed native SDK debouncer, live runtime-config reads, public pending/flush observations and genuine ingress claim lifecycles; account, sender and separate conversation identities are explicit, and helpers apply and restore channel-wide delays of 0, 1000 and 30000ms. Peer names do not select config. The fixture publicly declares messages.inbound as a live-read reload prefix and remains the same instance across the pending-config observation. This capability is unavailable until implemented (accountId, burstPeer, fixture, noDebouncePeer, sender)
- channel-delivery-queued: queued outbound deliveries pending on a slow Telegram chat (slowChatId)

A native canonical-profile redirect outside the owned clone is unavailable before mutation. No profile, home/default store, environment, credential, host or service preparation is performed to manufacture a different target.

- plugin-message-hooks: The existing supplied hook-only plugin and dormant marker/gate definitions are available but disabled between observations: authored plugins.entries[pluginId].enabled:false. It is separate from the conformance channel, mock providers, Discord/Telegram adapters and ambient controller, with its existing trusted permission configuration already supplied. Explicit --message-hooks owners temporarily enable it through native config.patch, capture registration/runtime adoption, run the original hook input, then join work and restore false through actual native registry replacement. api.on has no unregister handle; predicates alone never count as retirement. Keep all existing marker/account/peer and prepared-final keys. No new plugin installation, permission widening, account, secondary environment or product API is introduced. (accountId, cancelMarker, deferMarker, finalizeChatId, peer, rewriteMarker, telegramChatId, lifecycleFile). lifecycleFile: Nonsecret descriptor of the already installed hook-only fixture pluginId, expected installation/source identity, existing private fixture control/registration/disposal receipt channel and allowed case predicates. It contains no credential, source config backup, new product method, install command or foreign environment. Existing preparedFinalGateFile identifies this same plugin/controller, not a second global hook source.

- dm-scope-isolated: existing trait; these phases additionally read accountId (the same identifier-value rule applies)
- mock-provider-tool-calls: existing trait; these phases additionally read cardClearMarker, chatId, manyToolsMarker, progressMarker, restartMarker, slowFetchMarker (the same identifier-value rule applies)
- session-group-watched: existing trait; these phases additionally read groupId, senderPeer (the same identifier-value rule applies)
- session-main-rolling: existing trait; these phases additionally read accountId (the same identifier-value rule applies)
- session-progress-card: existing trait; these phases additionally read chatId (the same identifier-value rule applies)

Limits:
- Phases that drive Kova's conformance channel (support/plugins/kova-channel-probe) cannot record at this revision, because the plugin imports openclaw/plugin-sdk/channel-message, which OpenClaw removed in favour of openclaw/plugin-sdk/channel-outbound; they are written as they will run once the plugin is updated.
- In an isolated Kova home under /tmp with OCM 0.2.48 and Node 24.21.0, nine media and source-visible delivery rows of the probe scenario reported fewer deliveries than expected on OpenClaw 2026.9.9 and 2026.10.3, while the same code passed them with OCM 0.3.0 in an existing OCM store. The cause is not established (possibly /tmp to /private/tmp path resolution or the OCM version), so differences recorded by phases that drive media or source-visible delivery under such a setup are not yet attributable to the change.
- The template declares 123 product-command or unavailable-wrapper forms; only the Gateway-start helper is an existing callable script here. The command catalogue is absent in this checkout. The template is a derived plan, and these dependencies must be implemented and registered before it can record observations.
- Run 2 found 379/438 entry points, below the required nine-tenths share. The final condition-heading shares are 246/258 and 248/258, both above nine tenths; the finer condition-by-meaning shares are recorded separately in comparison.md. This reconciliation does not resolve the entry-enumeration acceptance gap.
- The background held-turn contract bounds admission separately from its lifetime and releases on join. Gateway restarts can interrupt that ambient condition; later phases must record interruption, and the dedicated restart flows create their own handles where recorded. Runtime continuity and cancellation have not been demonstrated.
- Repairing the conformance plugin import alone is insufficient for account, group, allowlist, mention and command-authorization observations. The present probe fixes default-account direct-chat facts and sets CommandAuthorized to true. The channel-probe-admission capability and --admission sdk wrapper path are additional unavailable prerequisites for those flows.
- The question, created-resource, configured-turn, debounce-overlap and suspension wrappers are declared orchestration, not implemented helpers. Their contracts include captured IDs, active lifetimes, configuration restoration and cancellation cleanup. The authored-fallback wrapper also needs access to the conformance runtime send context and an adapter that degrades data blocks to text.
- Supported action variants are kept separate: Telegram provides sticker search, Discord provides reaction listing and upload, iMessage provides the native attachment and message-effect actions, and Slack provides download. Those live adapters need real accounts; their phases remain outside reach while reachable sibling phases are retained. Matrix profile mutation is inventoried without an executable recipe because its shared remote baseline is not isolated across comparison clones. Matrix supplied-poll voting and Slack fixed-user conversation opening are inventoried without executable variants: the former shares one actor ballot and the latter opens or resumes the same remote conversation across comparison clones.
- Startup inspection overlap requires a verified task-owned schema-inspection child on a host supporting pause/resume signals. The wrapper must resume it on timeout or interruption and report an uncaptured interval as unavailable evidence. This is a bounded process-level scenario, not a promised OpenClaw inspection hook.
- The origin-subject SDK condition requires the additional captured-control capability. No bundled adapter at this revision exposes that exact event mode; the conformance flow must retain the creator binding and compare it through the actual ingress resolver.
- iMessage upload-file, fresh-message unsend and message effects, plus Slack download, remain live-account variants. Their adapter paths are selected explicitly. Matrix set-profile remains an uncovered entry without a mutation command. No whole operation becomes newly uncovered because the existing reachable send, poll, edit/delete, reaction, sticker and upload siblings remain. Matrix supplied-poll voting and Slack fixed-user conversation opening are inventoried without executable variants: the former shares one actor ballot and the latter opens or resumes the same remote conversation across comparison clones.
- Created live emoji, sticker, scheduled-event and file-upload IDs are recorded, but the current message-tool surface provides no corresponding cleanup command for every such object. These real-account flows remain outside this template's reach; their future execution must supply owned platform cleanup rather than pretend an unsupported delete action exists. Fixture-created objects remain confined to disposable transports.

Native Matrix identifiers are stored as file contents, because room and event sigils are not persona-key characters. The channel-real-accounts file keys matrixDmUnapprovedFile, matrixEncryptedRoomFile, matrixGatedRoomFile, matrixInviteRoomFile, matrixNameKeyedRoomFile, matrixPeerFile, matrixPerRoomDmFile, matrixPollArgsFile, matrixProxyRoomFile, matrixRoomFile, matrixRoomIdFile identify those files in each cloned environment. Each peer file contains the exact Matrix user or room identifier. matrixPollArgsFile contains a JSON object with the existing poll's native `to` and `pollId`; it contains no credentials. The tool wrapper merges these identifiers with the fixed poll action and account arguments.

Held-turn follow-ups are admitted in the background, then the predecessor is released and joined before the saved follow-up handles are joined. Live Discord thread flows retain the newly created thread ID through reply and cleanup; cleanup custody persists across interruption.

Outbound retry exhaustion uses the Gateway's existing five-second recovery scheduler and real five-attempt backoff, with a 1140000-millisecond observation deadline inside a slow 1200000-millisecond phase. The unavailable helper must capture the fresh delivery row, use a retryable non-dispatch fault that persists outside the Gateway across the restart variant, and record terminal settlement and later native observations. Fault removal on timeout first quiesces the owned clone so an orphaned delivery cannot be sent during cleanup. Existing short-lived fault counters do not supply this capability.

Routing tiers replace and restore the competing binding array. Positive fallback supplies a configured caller owner; the negative case runs in the separate explicit multi-agent clone with every owner source absent. The shared routing selectors retain that negative case when sibling routing paths change. Common tiers run once; the sibling retains only the direct wildcard variant and binding-level DM/group scope overrides. Twelve additional repeated atomic shim/probe scenarios are consolidated into their canonical flows, retaining dedicated event observations and the union of selection paths.

The Telegram shim supplies distinct synthetic tokens newAccountToken, tokenAccountToken and hotReloadToken for their corresponding created accounts. Promotion resolves its separate existing synthetic identity through the promotedCredential reference inside channel-account-add. They differ from each other and from every existing configured bot token; they are fixture identities, never real credentials. Live reaction fixtures retain a matching existing bot reaction for each removal, Signal uses a direct recipient and numeric message timestamp, and Feishu uses its native THUMBSUP type; other success reactions use Unicode 👍.

The two offline operations and queued-delivery restart run after ordinary observations. Each disruptive flow records the current ambient generation and its intentional interruption; later observations re-establish native custody, and the final ambient join follows all foreground observations. Scenario-specific overlap handles remain separate. Successful login during an account start uses the WhatsApp auth.login path and remains outside reach without the declared live fixture.

The new ingress monitor helpers are unavailable declarations using the installed native SDK and account queues. Nine of the ten added inspection conditions have account-free written flows after that fixture exists; older-host fallback is outside reach. No derived run is credited for these omissions. guardedPeer is reserved for the guarded recorder and its exact per-group binding, never ordinary dispatch. Claimed Telegram resubmission needs a genuine failed media update; a held model response cannot preserve its ingress claim.

Crash and lease observations now use an unavailable boundary wrapper that captures the matching native request, queue/producer ownership, persisted final or tool intent, or recovery/model state before signaling. It records an unavailable interval without signaling if the required boundary is missed, uses only harness response holds and read-only native state, and always resumes a paused owned process. The 30-second lease stall starts after an observed renewal with sufficient remaining lease time. Streaming configuration, provider scripts and holds are scoped and restored.

The unacknowledged-input condition uses the conformance manual-ack flow and its actual persisted input/ack callback state. The Telegram sibling records interrupted-turn recovery only; holding its model cannot establish native transport acknowledgement timing. Detached-completion recovery remains a real Codex-harness case with captured parent admission, outside reach here.

Pre-adoption shutdown and deferred-slot flows wait for native callback and claim evidence before their disruptive action. The Telegram deferred-followup case uses a held adopted predecessor only as a barrier; it must separately capture the later native deferred handoff. If that adapter observation is unavailable, conformance occupancy evidence does not substitute for it. Each whole case restores service/configuration and joins or cancels its owned work. Pending-state resubmission records its own native claim before the disruptive action; subsequent foreground observations re-establish ambient custody.

The lease-expiry flow differs from the short live-lease stall: it holds an actual native request and pauses beyond the captured owner expiry, then observes native renewal rejection/abort before releasing the response. A slow response alone does not expire a renewing lease. The duplicate no-heartbeat flow is consolidated into the slow pre-adoption watchdog case with all original selectors retained.

config-acp-binding additionally supplies discordPreparation, a file describing a dedicated disposable Discord binding and its initialized fixture backend session, with accepted and desired model/thinking settings. The unavailable native-binding wrapper invokes registered /help, captures the actual first backend control, revokes command access only while that control is pending, waits for adopted configuration and records subsequent controls separately. It restores configuration, settles held calls and closes only its own fixture session/backend. No product authorization callback is replaced.

channels-test-accounts also supplies lifecycleAccountId, a dedicated configured conformance account distinct from default, the ambient account and all selected background work. The unavailable overlap fixture must retain its actual callback/gate state per account and generation. It holds only that callback after native abort for less than the native stop grace, captures the still-pending stop, and records the competing start before releasing and restoring the account state.

Markdown table rendering observations use the native Telegram adapter with explicit account selection and scoped markdown.tables/richMessages settings. Peer names do not select a formatting mode. The separately configured block request with richMessages false exercises the native code-fallback path; raw conformance text is not counted as conversion. Discord scheduled-event CLI calls select external events and supply location, start and end times, matching the message-tool variants.

Account-owned variants explicitly select their configured account: reaction levels use the actual Telegram adapter guidance/action policy, DM account-map history selects accountMapAccountId, literal response prefixes use accountId, template prefixes use a separate templateAccountId, and disabled join introduction selects its Telegram account. Their peers belong to those configured accounts; peer names alone do not select policy. The detached-completion crash records the interrupted ambient generation; the next foreground phase must capture active custody again.

Additional configuration content:
The following keys identify accounts, agents or fixture files. Configuration fragments describe those entities; persona-key values remain simple identifiers or paths.

- toolProgressAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "progress", "progress": {"toolProgress": true}}}, "channel": "kova-channel-probe", "peerKey": "toolProgressPeer"}
- rawDetailAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "progress", "progress": {"commandText": "raw", "toolProgress": true}}}, "channel": "kova-channel-probe", "peerKey": "rawDetailPeer"}
- limitsAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "progress", "progress": {"label": "Kova", "maxLineChars": 40, "maxLines": 3, "toolProgress": true}}}, "channel": "kova-channel-probe", "peerKey": "limitsPeer"}
- partialToolProgressAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "partial", "preview": {"toolProgress": true}}}, "channel": "kova-channel-probe", "peerKey": "toolProgressPeer"}
- noEditAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "progress"}}, "adapterContract": "The future fixture provides no safe preview-edit callback for this account; execute the native/public SDK fallback and retain ordinary final delivery. Do not fabricate an edit failure or equate mode off with unavailable edit support.", "channel": "kova-channel-probe", "peerKey": "channel-probe-conversations:dmPeer"}
- Every new AccountId resolves independently through the future named-account conformance capability; peers do not select accounts. Account ids must not alias default/ambient state. Mode configs listed here are the effective merged settings, including inherited root values. block.enabled must be absent at root and account for explicitPreview. Each peer is explicitly bound to the listed agentId; no per-agent raw-detail or human-delay override may shadow a default variant. The fixture must pass AccountId to the actual shared block/preview resolvers; safe-edit and typing callbacks remain declared missing support.

config-telegram-reasoning-stream:
- accountId: Exact Telegram account serving chatId; select this bot identity at inbound transport, preserve AccountId through routing and outbound calls, with a unique synthetic shim token.
- accountId routes chatId to a dedicated agent whose reasoningDefault is stream; that session has no conflicting reasoningLevel and the sender is authorized for reasoning state. A peer name or preview mode alone does not enable reasoning.

session-progress-card:
- accountId: Exact Telegram account serving chatId; select this bot identity at inbound transport, preserve AccountId through routing and outbound calls, with a unique synthetic shim token.
- accountId is a Telegram account using progress mode, block false, toolProgress true; the declared existing progress-card session belongs to this exact account/peer route, not a different default session.

config-telegram-rich-messages:
- accountId: Exact Telegram account serving chatId; select this bot identity at inbound transport, preserve AccountId through routing and outbound calls, with a unique synthetic shim token.
- partialAccountId: Exact Telegram account serving partialChatId; select this bot identity at inbound transport, preserve AccountId through routing and outbound calls, with a unique synthetic shim token.
- accountId and partialAccountId both enable richMessages true; the partial account additionally has mode partial and block false. Capture actual accountConfig.richMessages before rendering.

config-block-streaming:
- telegramAccountId: Exact Telegram account for block mode, sharing access to the original specified peer and using a unique synthetic token.
- agentId: Dedicated owner selected by this exact account and peer.
- telegramAccountId uses mode partial with streaming.block.enabled true; block break and chunk defaults are configured by the scoped wrapper, not by account identity. All synthetic Telegram tokens are distinct across simultaneously configured accounts.

config-streaming-mode:
- partialAccountId: Exact Telegram account serving partialChatId; select this bot identity at inbound transport, preserve AccountId through routing and outbound calls, with a unique synthetic shim token.
- toolLogAccountId: Exact Telegram account for toolLog mode, sharing access to the original specified peer and using a unique synthetic token.
- partialToolProgressAccountId: Exact Telegram account for partialToolProgress mode, sharing access to the original specified peer and using a unique synthetic token.
- progressAccountId: Exact Telegram account for progress mode, sharing access to the original specified peer and using a unique synthetic token.
- discordPartialAccountId: {"accountConfig": {"streaming": {"block": {"enabled": false}, "mode": "partial"}}, "botIdentity": "distinct fixture-owned bot/account", "channel": "discord", "peer": "channel-discord-fixture:channelId"}
- Replace stale description "block streaming off, final only": partialAccountId uses streaming.mode partial and block.enabled false; toolLogAccountId uses progress mode, block false, progress.toolProgress true and bounded maxLines/maxLineChars; progressAccountId uses progress with block false and default initial delay; partialToolProgressAccountId uses partial with preview.toolProgress true. Peer keys remain unchanged and are accessible through their selected bot identity.

channel-typing-modes:
- accountId: Explicit conformance account serving the named peers; it supplies real typing callbacks.
- neverAgentId: Configured agent reached by an exact product account/peer binding for neverPeer.
- instantAgentId: Configured agent reached by an exact product account/peer binding for instantPeer.
- thinkingAgentId: Configured agent reached by an exact product account/peer binding for thinkingPeer.
- messageAgentId: Configured agent reached by an exact product account/peer binding for messagePeer.
- unsetAgentId: Dedicated agent reached by all unset-mode event peers as well as unsetPeer, with no per-agent typing override.
- telegramAccountId: Telegram fixture account serving the original DM/group event peers with actual typing support.
- The public typing owner is agents.entries[id].typingMode with agents.defaults.typingMode fallback. Account ids only select transport; explicit bindings must select each listed agent. Unset deletes both values; direct and mentioned-group fallback is instant, unmentioned group fallback is message. Heartbeat/system-event paths force never independently.

config-typing-agent-override:
- accountId: Telegram shim account serving chatId.
- agentId: Dedicated agent reached by chatId and accountId.

channel-telegram-shim:
- streamingScenarioAccountId: Dedicated Telegram account for scoped streaming/render scenario setup and restoration, distinct from ambient accounts.

channel-discord-fixture:
- streamingScenarioAccountId: Dedicated Discord account for scoped streaming/render scenario setup and restoration, distinct from ambient accounts.

channel-real-accounts:
- mattermostPreviewAccountId: Real mattermost account with effective streaming.mode partial and streaming.block.enabled false; existing target is reachable by this account. Outside current reach.
- matrixPreviewAccountId: Real matrix account with effective streaming.mode partial and streaming.block.enabled false; existing target is reachable by this account. Outside current reach.
- live-workplace-settings retains outside-reach classification. msteams resolves the actual single-account bot; Teams mode lives at channels.msteams.streaming.mode, with no invented accounts entry. The teamsBlockModeChannel is reachable by that bot and sender. The Mattermost account is configured/enabled and exclusively owned for scoped chatmode changes; mattermostOnCharChannel admits the sender, and the wrapper submits prefix ! after applying oncharPrefixes [!]. Missing access, bot lifecycle or config adoption yields unavailable without injection. Neither command mutates shared durable user environments.

Native Markdown table observations:
- The retained message-agent-streaming-states-2 Telegram flow selects code, bullets and off with richMessages false, block with richMessages true, and a separate requested block with richMessages false for native code fallback. Each call selects its actual existing account/peer, supplies the same controlled table content, captures adopted settings and joins native output before exact configuration restoration. The conformance adapter records text unchanged and supplies no Markdown table conversion evidence.

config-heartbeat-short:
- agentId: The existing short-cadence heartbeat owner retains its original meaning. These three manual-wake cases do not use or change that owner.
- manualWakeAgentId: Separate already configured owner, distinct from the ambient and short-cadence owners, with authored heartbeat every:0m before clone startup and an ordinary bounded provider response. Supply no wake, due cron monitor or queued system event for this owner before the cloned run's first owned case. No owner is provisioned by the helper. Its real target/account supplies the shared delivery case; the explicit target:none typing case remains internal.
- All three calls reuse manualWakeAgentId. Later cases require captured native terminal settlement, exact configuration restoration and successful ambient unpark from the preceding owned manual wake. Restore original every:0m between cases; do not require a never-before-woken owner again. If earlier work is unaccounted for, withhold the next wake and retain the existing failure barrier. A runtime every:0m adoption does not prove that a previous short-cadence retry queue was cleared.
- All three explicit wake invocations acquire the existing ambient controller's scoped park reservation, release and join its held work, then temporarily patch only this owner's heartbeat every to 0m and timeoutSeconds to 60. The native manual wake resolves its own configured session and retains all Main-lane, cron and recovery guards. Fixture custody supplies a real exclusive interval with no other due heartbeat, cron or uncontrolled foreground input, including work from the unchanged short-cadence agentId owner. If that cadence cannot supply the interval, the case is unavailable before input; the helper does not disable another owner. Another configured agent alone is not an idle-lane prerequisite. Busy native retries are not terminal settlement proof.
- Each case captures actual native wake/run and typing/delivery outcomes, joins or cancels only its captured work, restores exact authored heartbeat/typing values and absence through native config adoption, then releases its reservation and captures fresh active ambient work. An accepted or skipped wake is not run/delivery proof. Missing fixture/controller custody or an existing disposable-clone teardown owner stops before input. A valid native skip/failure remains observed execution output. Incomplete cleanup returns exit 2 and leaves the controller failed-parked; later ensure-active, unpark and park acquisitions refuse the same handle until clone teardown, without requiring a new phase-dependency API.
- Native typing condition 400 stays partial, and conditions 466–468 remain outside reach. This lifecycle correction adds no visibility, route or cancellation variant.

Native scenario configuration:
Each named fixture scenario owns adoption, completion and restoration of these settings before returning.
