Pairing fixture content:
- channel-pairing-fixtures: A nonsecret fixture manifest containing the initial retention cohort and independently owned per-case accounts, synthetic sender/peer identities and expected ownership facts. Retention has at least one genuinely expired canonical request and more than three currently unexpired canonical requests on the same configured conformance account, with distinct lastSeenAt values and enough remaining TTL for the bounded observation; this is a supplied schema-valid compatibility fixture, not something the helper creates by bypassing native admission. Manifest identifies its exclusive rows and the unknown fourth sender. Later cases have no stored live codes: use dedicated configured pairing accounts/senders, initially absent requests/allow entries, explicit account routing, and real native creation/capture just before consumption. Telegram accounts use their own distinct synthetic fixture tokens already provisioned through the persona. UI approval/dismissal targets and offline/refusal targets are disjoint from all other cases. Owner-bootstrap scenarios save/restore global owner configuration rather than assuming it remains absent after another approval. No live credentials in the manifest. (manifest)
- channel-pairing-cap: Distinguish the initial greater-than-three unexpired same-account pruning fixture from the ordinary exactly-three admission-cap state. Three rows alone cannot show excess pruning. Both are observed in initial-retention: allowlisted pairing projection then native list to three, then one fourth-sender request before any approval frees a slot.
- channel-pairing-pending: Its aged/static request rows are exclusively initial-retention inputs. Retire code/pendingPeer/secondRequestId/thirdRequestId as live approval/repeat targets; later helpers capture fresh native request identities in their own case.
- channel-dm-pairing: Retain policy/account fixtures and the dedicated allowlist test sender. Retire pendingCode, offlineCode, uncertainReplyCode, pendingRequestId and pendingSender as cross-phase consumption inputs; expiredCode, if retained, belongs only to the initial read-only retention snapshot and subsequent rejection attempt. Fresh case identities come from channel-pairing-fixtures. Do not reuse unknownPeer or unknownUserId between pairing, start, callback and approval cases.
- channel-ambient-turn: Setup uses its already-authorized dedicated account/sender and must not upsert a pairing request before initial-retention. If startup has already consumed retention data, initial-retention reports unavailable rather than recording an already-pruned list as pruning.

Pairing retention is observed immediately after setup, before any selected Channels UI or ordinary pairing consumer. An allowlisted before projection must show the supplied expired and excess canonical cohort without retaining pairing codes or metadata bodies. Later approval, repeat, dismissal, callback, refusal and offline cases capture fresh native requests in their own bounded lifecycles; preloaded live codes are not reused. Missing or previously consumed retention state is unavailable, not evidence of a pruning transition.

The live preview cases require mattermostPreviewFixtureFile and matrixPreviewFixtureFile in channel-real-accounts, plus dedicated mattermostPreviewAccountId and matrixPreviewAccountId. Files contain only nonsecret identity/capability facts, owned native anchor captures and configured/named credential references. The supplied routes have partial streaming, block output disabled, automatic visible replies and no preview-disabling modifiers; Matrix also has replyToMode all and threadReplies off. The provider fixture must emit a correlated partial and hold its final; Matrix media-only final additionally requires native tool-progress output followed by media-only terminal content. These extensions are unavailable. Mattermost deletion targets only the captured new bot preview. Matrix reply-target mismatch and media-only redaction are separate; the error-final variant remains unexercised. Native cleanup is recorded separately from product redaction, and unrelated history is never retained.

Acknowledgement and reaction fixture content:
- channel-group-allowlisted also supplies accountId for its Telegram groupChatId; its mentioned acknowledgement observation explicitly adopts global messages.ackReactionScope group-mentions and nonempty account ackReaction.
- Scope, lifecycle status and usage defaults are shared settings, so the configured-turn wrapper changes them serially, captures adoption, joins the complete turn and restores exact prior values. Separate peer names never imply different simultaneous global settings. Off and minimal reaction-level observations select their actual Telegram accounts. The existing channel-reaction-level minimalAccountId/minimalPeer pair is reused.

Scripted marker content for channel-streaming-modes:
- mediaRepeatMarker: The scripted turn sends one fixture-owned media reference in an early native streamed block, then its terminal payload repeats that exact media identity with additional substantive final text. Keep the media identity stable so native duplicate-media filtering, rather than two different assets, is observed. Record the first native media delivery before releasing terminal content. Uses blockStreamingAccountId with blockStreamingPeer and the routed agentId. MEDIA references and final-text scripts exist, but guaranteed early-delivery-before-terminal ordering needs the declared controlled provider/transport fixture. A literal marker or repeated text alone does not establish the interval.
- duplicateFinalMarker: A nonempty scripted assistant answer is emitted through normal provider SSE text deltas and is repeated exactly, without an added prefix, suffix or new media, in the provider terminal aggregate. Choose enough content to exercise the configured native text_end block path and record its actual streamed block before judging final duplicate handling. Do not issue two separate user turns or two unrelated sends. Uses blockStreamingAccountId with blockStreamingPeer and the routed agentId. The installed provider final-text renderer supplies SSE deltas and an aggregate of the same text. Per-case script selection and observation still belong to the unavailable wrapper; no invented chunk or final-repeat response type is needed.
- mediaFinalMarker: A correlated visible/pending partial preview is established first, then the terminal reply consists of one fixture-owned media attachment and no ordinary final text. Capture the actual pending preview before terminal release; record native cancellation/finalization and media delivery. Do not replace this with an ordinary media reply whose preview never existed. Uses partialAccountId with partialPeer and the routed agentId. A simple MEDIA final-text script is insufficient to guarantee preceding preview and a distinct media-only terminal result. This requires the declared controlled streaming/final fixture; absent it, report unavailable evidence. Preserve mode partial and block disabled.
- slowFetchMarker: The script calls the registered web_fetch tool with a bounded fixture-owned HTTP(S) resource admitted by the native fetch policy. Hold the actual fetch response briefly after tool invocation while native typed tool-progress callbacks run, then release a successful bounded result and finish with a substantive final reply. Record the real tool name, invocation/result and progress lifecycle, not text imitating a tool. Uses partialToolProgressAccountId with toolProgressPeer and the routed agentId. tool-calls can express web_fetch, but provider response delay is not a slow tool: the owned HTTP response gate must exist and be cleaned up. No external URL or policy bypass is introduced by this declaration. Keep preview.toolProgress true on the selected partial-preview account.
- progressCardMarker: Use the actually offered progress_card tool with a valid meaningful two-step card on the selected progressAccountId/progressPeer/routed agent. Capture native successful tool result, stored card revision and actual plan/draft update or its absence. Then use a valid call with empty markdown and plan to clear the same owned card; capture native card state and draft cleanup while preserving other real activity. This recipe covers condition 376 only. It does not attempt or credit a native progressCard.put failure: no supported owner-scoped public or OS fault fixture is supplied. Condition 377 remains outside reach. Do not substitute a Telegram edit error, invalid tool arguments, synthetic tool result, private hook or database mutation for a failed native plan write.
- silentAfterPreviewMarker: The scripted turn creates one observable partial preview, then ends normally with the product silent terminal result and no final text/media delivery or provider error. Capture preview ownership before terminal release so the unused-preview cleanup branch is distinguished from error-final retention. Do not assume that sending only NO_REPLY creates a preview. Uses partialAccountId with partialPeer and the routed agentId. Guaranteed preview followed by a different silent terminal result requires controlled provider/preview lifecycle support beyond a bare final-text NO_REPLY script. Its absence is unavailable evidence, not successful coverage.
All six keys are trigger identifiers. The scoped fixture selects the corresponding script for the actual account/peer/agent and invocation, records that correlation, and restores only its own script/fault/gate after completion or cancellation. It must not globally reset a provider serving the ambient held turn or other accounts. Persona marker selection is a future fixture contract, not an undocumented capability of write-mock-ai-provider-script.mjs. No new dependency or provider version is proposed.

Existing scripted marker details:
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressMarker: A valid multi-step native progress_card update plus actual bounded tool work and later progress update before a substantive final; used by the existing progress rendering and separately armed fault/hook cases. It does not arm or create their platform faults itself.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:slowFetchMarker: The same native web_fetch response-gated sequence as channel-streaming-modes.slowFetchMarker, using the explicitly selected Telegram partialToolProgressAccountId and its existing peer; never alias the accounts merely because both keys have the same suffix.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:cardClearMarker: The same native create/capture/clear recipe described for progressCardMarker, on the explicitly selected Telegram toolLogAccountId and actual session. It covers successful clearing only; no write-failure action or credit is claimed. Condition 377 needs a separate genuinely supported native progressCard.put failure fixture and remains outside reach.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:manyToolsMarker: More successful native tool-progress lines than that selected account effective maxLines, with at least one real tool argument/output line longer than effective maxLineChars, followed by a substantive final. Use meaningful fixture operations; do not model progress by printed fake callback text.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restartMarker: Invoke the actually registered Gateway restart operation through the native agent tool path from the exact Telegram route, preserve its native restart sentinel/return routing and record resumption; no script-only success text substitutes for a restart.
- mock-provider-media-reply:marker: The consuming case selects a scoped media script: Telegram block-streaming duplication uses the early-media/same-media-plus-new-text sequence, while the Discord partial-preview case requires captured preview followed by a media-only final. A single unconditional script must not be claimed to establish both different states.
- channel-probe-conversations:longReplyMarker: A scripted nonempty reply exceeding the selected transport text limit, using the existing long-reply fixture and normal native chunk/preview handling.
- channel-probe-conversations:silentMediaMarker: The terminal reply carries a fixture-owned audio attachment together with the product silent token; preserve both native media directives and token handling.
- channel-probe-conversations:runnerFailMarker: A real scoped runner/provider failure, not returned text saying failure. Optional/required group cases fail without earlier visible output; preview-final cases first capture the actual partial preview, then produce the native accepted error-final path. Case-specific preparation is required; an unconditional immediate error cannot establish the preview cases.
- config-visible-replies-tool:strandMarker: A substantive final assistant answer without any message-tool call under the existing tool-only route, leaving native stranded-final recovery to act.
- config-visible-replies-tool:doubleStrandMarker: The first substantive final omits the message tool and the native recovery follow-up also returns a substantive final without that tool; correlate both native attempts to one original turn rather than generating two unrelated inputs.

Queued successor fixture content:
- channel-plugin-binding also supplies queuedCancelAccountId and queuedCancelThreadId: a dedicated conformance account/thread with its real core-managed fixture-plugin binding and admitted senderPeer, isolated from the ambient turn and other queued cases. Setup uses the supported approval-driven plugin binding API; the two bounded inbound_claim holds, actual request AbortSignal forwarding and correlated callback observations are unavailable fixture capabilities.
The cancellation case aborts only the successor request, records predecessor completion, and starts reset against the captured actual session identity while the successor claim remains pending. It neither fabricates a followup row nor uses a model hold as a plugin claim. All cleanup is owned by one bounded invocation; no hardcoded main-session database path is used.

Ambient activity lifetime:
The initial setup captures a native held provider request and the matching recovery claim in the same Gateway as the primary observations. Each foreground phase checks that actual generation through the original handle. After an interrupted or completed generation is settled, its sole owner may admit one replacement on demand; the presence of a handle alone never proves activity. Missing custody or an unsettled prior execution returns exit 2 and leaves the dependent phase incomplete. Configuration-capable helpers repeat this bounded check after adoption and restoration, preserving the separate ambient binding only when it is provably disjoint from the scenario. Shared reloads and explicit interruptions are recorded, not hidden. There is no autonomous re-admission loop and no product timeout extension.
The ambient account, route, sender and provider capacity are separate from every ordinary scenario. channel-probe-conversations:accountId differs from channel-ambient-turn:accountId, including the explicit single-account stop/start operation. The initial sender is already authorized and never consumes pairing-retention fixtures. The model-pin reload case restarts its selected ordinary account after the patch before admitting its next input.
All foreground observations, including late account removal and logout, precede the final ambient join and platform teardown. Scenario-owned background work is joined beside its observation before another scenario can interrupt it. The missing helper owns cancellation and executor-failure cleanup even if the final phase is not reached.
Separately supplied persona environments are cloned by the executor under the existing contract. A primary-clone ambient turn does not establish concurrency in those other Gateways. Their isolated fresh-install, missing-owner and routing-error variants record their stated condition without claiming busy-instance evidence; a future busy comparison there requires its own already authorized ambient content and captured custody. No extra clone or inferred owner is introduced here.

Reaction notification fixture content:
- channel-telegram-shim:reactionNotificationAccountId: A dedicated configured Telegram account on the existing shim, with a unique synthetic bot identity/token, access to dmChatId and memberSenderId, and an admitted non-bot sender. Exclusive custody allows serial reactionNotifications own/all/off changes and exact restoration without changing default/ambient account settings. Bot-owned notification anchors are created through native outbound delivery in each scenario, not supplied as guessed ids.
- channel-discord-fixture:reactionNotificationAccountId: A dedicated configured Discord fixture account/bot with access to the existing guildId/channelId and the two notification actor IDs. Its account-scoped guild entry is exclusively owned for temporary reactionNotifications allowlist plus users/roles changes; do not set reactionNotifications at the account top level.
- channel-discord-fixture:notificationAllowedUserId: A non-bot fixture guild member listed by native stable user ID in the selected guild/channel access restrictions for the allowlist case; never reused as a kick/ban/timeout subject.
- channel-discord-fixture:notificationExcludedUserId: A distinct non-bot fixture guild member absent from the selected users allowlist and every admitted role, with no permissive channel override; also disjoint from moderation and ambient participants. Its rejection is recorded at the actual native stage.
reaction-notify-own/all/off save, apply and restore the selected Telegram account reactionNotifications field. reaction-notify-allowlist runs on Discord and scopes reactionNotifications under the selected account guild entry, with stable users/roles membership and actual accessible guild/channel inputs. These fields are unrelated to reactionLevel guidance, actions.reactions tool gating, or messages.ackReactionScope acknowledgements.
Signal-specific inbound notifications still require a real Signal account; Discord fixture coverage of condition 500 does not substitute for condition 435.

Process completion fixture content:
- mock-provider-tool-calls:polledCompletionMarker: Identifier-safe trigger for the exact scoped exec/process script; not a natural-language instruction.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:polledCompletionScript: Path to the script contract with actual exec arguments, dynamic process-id binding, bounded native terminal polls and final response only after consumption.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:lateOperationScript: Path to the scoped public onItemEvent producer script for the older/current/older-end progress sequence.
- mock-provider-tool-calls: The provider script controller must read the actual prior tool response before constructing subsequent arguments. Existing static completionToolCalls substitutions do not capture a dynamically generated process id; this capture capability is explicitly missing support. Scope scripts to the foreground case so ambient held turns cannot consume their responses.
- mock-provider-tool-calls: Execution must have an already available Node command in the selected native tool environment, with exec and process permitted by the existing policy. The child emits the unique nonempty marker; verify effective notifyOnExit true. A policy/host/tool prerequisite failure is unavailable before launch, not permission to weaken policy.
- native-mcp-completion:clientFile: Path to an existing authorized disposable native loopback MCP client configuration or credential reference; contains actual endpoint/protocol and session/agent scope, never inline secrets in persona values.
- native-mcp-completion: Requires an already available native OpenClaw MCP loopback endpoint and a valid native-issued scope matching the captured channel source session, plus actual exec/process discovery. No Codex runtime, real channel account or new external service is inferred. Do not mint a synthetic grant or bypass the native request/context checks. If absent, only the MCP phase is withheld/unavailable; embedded condition 643 remains selectable.
The polled-completion script launches the existing Node tool-host command that waits five seconds and prints a unique nonempty fixture marker, using exec yieldMs 10 and timeoutSeconds 15. It captures the generated process ID and issues process action poll with timeout 10000 until the real terminal result is consumed, within a total 30000-millisecond collection bound. Embedded consumption follows normal tool-result persistence; native MCP uses initialized discovery and a fully received projected HTTP response. After terminal consumption and foreground settlement, the shared heartbeat-wake custody owner parks and joins ambient work, then sends one later wake with mode now, nonempty scoped text and the exact captured source agent/session. The source heartbeat cadence0/timeout60 is supplied before clone startup, so no intervening configuration adoption can clear or change completion work. Actual wake/run/delivery settlement precedes script/config restoration and fresh ambient unpark. Retryable busy skips retain custody; unresolved work returns exit 2 with the controller failed-parked until owned-clone teardown. Both embedded and MCP callers use this same owner and explicit polledCompletionWakeFile scope. Cleanup never substitutes process remove for terminal-poll evidence. The tool host must already provide Node and a compatible shell; policy is not weakened.
The embedded case covers condition 643 independently. The mapped OpenClaw #138188 projection path uses event-polled-completion-mcp and cannot receive runtime credit from an embedded-only run. Missing existing MCP endpoint, grant or tool access withholds that variant without changing embedded selection. The late Telegram progress-item completion case has its own declared public onItemEvent producer sequence; it is distinct from process completion notifications.

Breaker and restoration fixture content:
- channels-test-accounts:restartAccountsFile: a nonsecret manifest of already configured task-owned channel/account identities, including the ordinary conformance account, separate ambient account and native fixture accounts. Intersect it with the original native active set and current configuration; exclude accounts intentionally stopped, disabled, removed, logged out or lacking their existing credentials. It authorizes no discovered or unrelated account.
Gateway health, manual account availability and native automatic-recovery eligibility are distinct observations. The interruption helper records native boot suppression before any explicit account starts, waits the actual native recovery deadline and effective monitor cadence when required, and restores only eligible exact accounts. Manual start never clears the breaker or counts as automatic startup. Native-recovery cases preserve the entire suppressed interval without manual starts, extra restarts or ambient re-admission; cleanup restoration is recorded afterward.
Condition 637 uses a supplied, genuinely native tombstoned-session fixture through event-tombstoned-session-reminder. The helper validates the dedicated account/route, exact sessionId and recovery cycleId, native terminal metadata and an unused first-reminder interval, then records two distinct ordinary human inputs in one Gateway process. The native admission rejection produces the reminder; the second input observes process-local suppression without resetting the session or cache. This run does not produce recovery-budget exhaustion. The fixture and helper are unavailable until supplied, and no session rows, timestamps or recovery counters may be manufactured. Slow timeout bounds reserve native-window time conditionally; there is no fixed delay, clock change or counter reset. The ambient guard remains non-mutating. Delivery-exhaustion quarantine remains stopped for disposal and is never restarted for cosmetic restoration.

WhatsApp reaction replacement remains inventoried but has no executable recipe on the supplied message. The account/message/participant tuple is shared across comparison clones; adding then clearing a reaction does not preserve an independent starting slot.

- session-tombstoned: an exclusively reserved conformance account, authorized human sender and peer routed to one configured main session whose genuine native recovery tombstone already exists in the disposable environment, with failed terminal state, exact sessionId/cycleId, native creation evidence, allowed outbound send policy, usable channel delivery, modelSelectionLocked false or absent, no active/pending recovery or delivery owner, no replacement/reset/deletion and no earlier ordinary inbound to that session in the current Gateway process; fixtureFile contains only its nonsecret identities and provenance references. This native fixture is not currently supplied and must not be created by database edits, clock changes, backdating or repeated successful resumptions. (fixtureFile)

Thaw fixture content:
- channels-test-accounts:thawIdlePolicyFile: nonsecret ownership facts for the task-owned heartbeat settings and agent IDs plus an observation window with no due cron job or unrelated work. The helper may temporarily set only those native heartbeat every fields to 0m and restores exact values afterward; unrelated work is never cancelled.
Both thaw modes temporarily park and join the ambient controller, establish genuine native idle through a released suspend preflight, and observe a fresh native tick before a 90000-millisecond interruption. Native timing logs establish the qualifying gap of at least 75000 milliseconds. CPU mode requires real bounded synchronous work in the Gateway process and measured native CPU classification; an external CPU hog or a held model is not equivalent. The helper resumes any paused process, restores owned configuration/accounts and captures renewed ambient custody in bounded cleanup.
Condition 267 also has a separate slow retry-expiry flow. It holds one genuine awaited conformance Gateway request as native root work through a low-CPU thaw, captures actual active-work deferral, and observes the full native ten-minute retry window before releasing the request and recording two idle ticks. Its 960000-millisecond bound covers the real interval; no model deadline is extended, clock changed or private activity counter manufactured. Condition 268 remains the distinct CPU-load branch. These controller, same-process CPU and finite request-holder capabilities are unavailable support.

Condition 427 retains its documented expired-or-replaced contract. The existing native-send-expired-lease flow records lease expiry and loss of ownership; it does not establish replacement by another producer. The former event-producer-claim-replaced recipe has been removed because its inbound completed before the process pause and its generic fault supplied no native replacement mechanism. The replaced-owner variant is outside reach until a real independently scheduled native owner and the stale caller's reservation boundary can both be observed for the same queue entry. No new runtime, direct queue mutation or speculative fixture is declared. Condition 427 remains partial. Condition 377 is outside reach because its native write-failure fixture is missing. Condition 589 is also outside reach without a supported automatic reconciliation adapter. These qualifications are included in the current coverage total at the start of this document; inventory and discovery shares are unchanged.

Tool-dependent fixture recipes:
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restrictedExecMarker: Select a scoped provider script attempting one harmless registered exec call (for example fixture-only printf) under the ACTUAL admitted sender context, then a final reply. Record advertised tools, requested call and native denial/result. A blocked/absent tool is the intended policy observation, not permission to add tools or override authorization.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:messageSendMarker: Select one normal model-facing message tool action send addressed through the actual bound source account/conversation, then a silent final to avoid a second visible send. Record offered tool catalog, exact tool-call identity and native denial or delivery receipt. If ingress denies the input or the tool is unavailable, preserve that actual outcome; never inject a fabricated accepted call or bypass policy.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:approvalPromptMarker: Select approvalPromptScript, whose harmless exec request uses the native ask always option and captures the real generated pending approval/prompt before scoped denial/cancellation and final completion. A policy denial without an approval record is not an approval-prompt observation.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restrictedDelegateMarker: Select restrictedDelegateScript for the actual restricted source sender; this is a multi-step native spawn and queued-followup recipe, not a text instruction to delegate.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restrictedDelegateScript: A fixture recipe for channel-inbound-turn: on the actual restricted source turn call sessions_spawn with a native hidden subagent run and cleanup delete, capture returned childSessionKey/runId and actual child execution, and have that child attempt the harmless restricted exec probe. Keep only this parent provider response active, admit one followup from the same restricted source sender on the same native conversation, and capture its queued operation identity before releasing parent/child work. Join child and parent through the native lifecycle (including sessions_yield when waiting for parent-targeted completion), then join the queued restricted followup executing the same exec probe. Only afterward send one native input from --after-sender on the same conversation with the harmless exec probe, recording its independent offered-tool and execution policy. Parent, child and followup provider routes/scripts are separately correlated, never one shared positional counter. If spawn itself is denied, record that denial and mark child/followup propagation evidence unavailable rather than enabling it. Cancel/join only owned operations and children and restore scripts in finally; never use /stop on the shared conversation as cleanup.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:approvalPromptScript: Native exec tool-calls recipe with a harmless fixture-only command and ask always. Capture tool-call id and actual approval id/prompt from native observations; record the pending interval, then resolve only this owned approval through existing authorized approval APIs and join the tool result/final. The supplied fixture must already permit creation/handling of that native approval; do not widen exec policy, approve an unrelated request, forge an approval result or leave the turn waiting beyond its command bound.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:agentRestartScript: A genuine existing System-agent delegation/host-authority recipe. The ordinary gateway tool supports config.get, config.schema.lookup and update.run, NOT restart. Use the actually offered openclaw tool action gateway_restart only inside its real host-owned System-agent context: capture the exact staged operation, exercise the existing host-verified approval/delegation policy, and capture host application plus actual restart/sentinel/outcome on the originating channel. Model-supplied approved true alone is insufficient. No shell lifecycle workaround, update.run side effect, injected tool exposure, synthetic host flags or service restart by the harness may substitute. If the supplied channel cannot reach this existing authorized context, report unavailable before claiming restart; keep the condition. Preserve account/ambient recovery custody and scoped cleanup.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressScript: The file-backed recipe for the already declared progressMarker: meaningful native progress_card steps, at least two bounded actual tool invocations and a substantive final. Use separate real call identities/results and native progress callbacks, not strings printed to look like progress. Scoped command/provider holds must fit the existing scenario deadline.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressYieldScript: Create native progress_card state, call sessions_spawn for a captured hidden subagent, then use the actual sessions_yield lifecycle while that child runs bounded scripted work; record real parent/child operation ids and progress/draft writes before child completion resumes the parent. No fake completion event or unrelated child may satisfy the boundary. Join or cancel owned child work and restore provider scripts in finally.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:reactionToolScript: The default Discord recipe scripts a registered message react action against a freshly captured fixture-owned Discord message in normal native tool context, recording call id, result/receipt and tool tracking. The explicit telegram-reaction-level branch runs in the real source turn admitted by channel-configured-turn. Capture that fresh Telegram inbound numeric message id, selected account/chat and actual native model request. Record only the channel reaction-guidance classification present in that request (absent, minimal or extensive), without exporting the complete prompt. Once the offered native message react action is available, script exactly one valid react request with emoji 🎉, bound to that captured account/chat/message; do not guess an old id or bypass action discovery. Capture native tool-call id, soft denial or success, actual setMessageReaction requests and normal terminal completion. No retry of a denied action. Keep actions.reactions enabled, acknowledgement emoji 👍, messages.ackReactionScope direct and status reactions disabled identically across all four cases, distinguishing acknowledgement requests from the scripted agent action. These independent settings do not imply that reactionLevel itself causes an acknowledgement. Observe native off/ack gating and minimal/extensive guidance without asserting frequency or a successful reaction. Missing supported script/request-observer capability is unavailable before input; after valid admission, absent guidance, denied action or unexpected platform output is product evidence. Join the turn, clear only a captured case-owned reaction when one exists through ordinary fixture/native cleanup, then restore exact configuration and ambient custody within the existing command bound. The existing Discord trackToolCalls recipe is unchanged; this branch adds no provider protocol field or product hook.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressThenErrorScript: Perform real native progress/tool work first and capture the progress draft, then make the correlated subsequent provider response fail through the supported error response. Record native error-final handling. An immediate provider error before any captured progress does not establish this case.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:heldProgressScript: Perform native progress/tool work, capture the created native draft, hold this operation terminal response, inject the scenario second message only after that boundary, then release and join in the same scope. Do not infer a draft from elapsed time.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:discordActionGroupsScript: For each explicitly configured native Discord action gate in this named fixture case, issue a corresponding model-facing message tool call using actual owned message/channel identifiers and capture the native allowed/denied result. Never call an adapter function directly and report it as a tool-policy observation. Preserve configured gating and restore only scoped fixture state.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:messageToolAbsentScript: Supply a substantive final on the actual ambient/tool-only route after capturing that the native model tool catalog lacks message. Preserve the native fallback/silence behavior; do not make up a message-tool result or enable the missing tool.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:quietFillerScript: Existing provider script for narration-filler and its two guard siblings. Emit actual primary assistant commentary/preamble and an allowed bounded exec Node --version call, capture native preamble/tool activity plus a successful Discord progress-draft receipt, then hold only the primary final across at least25000ms from the last actual preamble. The valid configured utility route produces a short native narration response in the positive case; negative cases preserve the same primary producer and observe actual request/edit absence or presence. Never replace native callbacks with fake activity. Release/cancel/join all work within 120000ms.
- channel-native-agent-restart: A supplied actual channel-to-System-agent/host-authority route that offers the native openclaw gateway_restart operation with its genuine proposal/delegation/approval lifecycle, existing required permission and origin routing. Do not expose the restricted tool to a normal agent, forge approved host state or install another runtime. If this context is absent, the planned agent-requested-restart observation is unavailable rather than replaced by a harness restart. (no keys)
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restartMarker: Select agentRestartScript and its actual authorized native System-agent/host route; the ordinary gateway tool has no restart action. Capture applied restart/sentinel and origin result, or unavailable context. This tightens the existing key definition without changing its value.
- channel-groups-mixed: Restricted-delegation fixture must already allow the native sessions_spawn path while retaining the intended restricted tool policy, and keep parent, child and queued followup on captured native source contexts. The later ownerSender uses its actual independent policy. If the supplied policy denies spawning, preserve that result and record missing downstream propagation evidence; never rewrite policy for a positive result.
- channel-real-accounts: The live Slack threaded and top-level progress inputs now use the existing progressMarker on that actual account/provider route; they remain outside reach without their existing live account, allowed native tools and owned scoped provider script. No timeout or permission widening.
Named and marker-selected recipes capture actual offered tools, calls, native results or policy denials, with dynamic identities taken from real results. Free-form instructions do not select mock scripts. Restricted delegation captures child execution and queued followup before the later sender; progress, approval and restart observations wait for their own native activity. No tool, permission, host flag or approval is fabricated to force a positive result. All required scripts and orchestration remain unavailable support.

The proactive QQ Bot case uses channel-real-accounts:qqbotInactivePeer, a dedicated valid test recipient whose supplied native/platform history establishes the required absence of recent interaction. It is separate from qqbotPeer and every inbound scenario; fixture setup and earlier phases must not message or simulate interaction from it. The case performs only native outbound message send on the selected real account, preserving policy errors as observations. No timestamp is rewritten, and the live-account case remains outside reach.

Content-dependent fixture recipes:
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:htmlMarker: Identifier-safe selector for final-text containing literal <b>bold</b> and <i>italic</i> outside code plus a unique harmless suffix. The scoped controller records exact produced fixture text; the native adapter performs conversion or literal handling. SMS still uses its supplied real account and remains outside reach.
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:finalTagMarker: Identifier-safe selector for final-text containing <final>KOVA_FINAL_OK</final> outside code, with ordinary configured model tag policy retained; record the raw fixture text and resulting native visible text, not text that merely describes tags.
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:shortTextMarker: Identifier-safe selector for one immediate final-text KOVA_SHORT_OK with no tool calls, media, reasoning or added delay. Confirm the actual selected progress-mode account and observed absence/presence of native draft; do not turn this into a fabricated draft assertion.
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:renderRecipesFile: File-backed named content recipes: reply-inline-html emits literal inline HTML; reply-spoiler emits ||KOVA_SPOILER||; reply-details-rich/plain emit identical <details><summary>Summary</summary>Body</details> text under their existing native account settings; reply-markdown-image emits ![fixture](fixture-owned-image-URL) using the already supplied served media, never a new public fetch; suppress-embeds emits an actual fixture-owned HTTP(S) link while native suppressEmbeds is active; html-parse-error-fallback emits supported bold/link markup and lets the already scoped platform fault reject the actual HTML request before the native plain-text fallback. Record selected scenario, exact nonsecret fixture content, actual native requests and final result. If required fixture media/URL is absent, the case is unavailable. The file explicitly carries the existing owned URL and its fixture media identity; no unnamed URL key, assumed local media server or arbitrary public URL is inferred. Missing served media is an unavailable prerequisite.
- mock-provider-long-reply:renderRecipesFile: Named final-text recipes: tall-reply contains effective maxLinesPerMessage + 2 nonempty lines; reply-formatted-chunks contains valid styled Markdown, lists, a quote and a fenced code block with total length above the selected Telegram text limit. Record resolved bounds, exact fixture text/hash and native chunks. Size limits are read, not rewritten.

- channel-broadcast-group:digestMarker: Identifier-safe marker while retaining real @agentA/@agentB mention text. Select digestScript for their two actual provider routes.
- channel-broadcast-group:digestScript: Each actually selected participant emits a distinct 5,000-character non-silent final through final-text, then a bounded non-silent continuation. Capture native participant, round, source identity, post-hook final length and the next native sibling digest. MAX_FINAL_CHARS is 4,000. With only two participants this establishes per-participant truncation; aggregate 16,000-character overflow remains outside this fixture and must not be claimed. No additional agent, changed round/turn limit or synthesized continuation request.
- channel-broadcast-group:roundScript: For each actually selected participant/round, provide distinct non-silent final-text. Wait for the native configured second round and record its real internal continuation identity and sibling digest; never inject a second human input as a continuation. Scope provider responses by native route/participant/round, not a shared positional counter.
- channel-broadcast-group:budgetScript: Produce bounded non-silent final-text for each actual participant invocation until the already configured maxTurns stops native execution; record count and stop reason. Do not silently reduce maxTurns or force all-pass early termination. Existing native bounds must fit the command deadline or this fixture is unavailable.
- channel-broadcast-group:heldRoundScript: Provide non-silent first-round text where needed, then hold one actual provider request of the named native broadcast root/participant/round. Background startup returns only after capturing that request and active native round. The later existing /stop or Gateway restart acts on that genuine interval. The existing saved-handle wait joins all owned participant work and releases exact held responses; interruption cleanup does the same. No arbitrary sleep or acceptance-only admission, no shared ambient hold.
- channel-broadcast-group:participantFailureScript: Identify the designated failing participant from the existing failingAgentGroup fixture before admission. Supply its actual model request a supported provider error while each other selected participant gets a bounded non-silent final-text response. Capture all real participant identities and native terminal dispositions; never fail the ambient provider or all participants by resetting a shared script. Restore scoped scripts and join/cancel owned work within the original bound.
- channel-streaming-modes:explicitReplyFinalMarker: Identifier-safe selector for explicitReplyFinalScript; --reply-to inbound is only input metadata and never substitutes for a final reply directive.
- channel-streaming-modes:explicitReplyFinalScript: Missing controlled provider/final producer: first obtain and capture a native partial preview, then release a final containing the native explicit reply directive [[reply_to:ACTUAL_CAPTURED_MESSAGE_ID]] plus substantive text for the same turn. Record parsed final replyTo identity and cancellation/finalization of that preview. The actual id is captured, never the literal token above. No preview means unavailable interval; ordinary final-text cannot by itself guarantee this ordering.
- channel-streaming-modes:previewFinalMarker: Identifier-safe selector for previewFinalScript, a preview-to-final lifecycle rather than a request for a short answer.
- channel-streaming-modes:previewFinalScript: Missing controlled stream fixture: emit sufficient ordinary text deltas to produce one native partial preview, capture actual message/preview identity, then complete the same final content while within the native edit-in-place limit. Record the actual final edit and terminal ownership. No assumed preview based on elapsed time; preserve account configuration and existing deadline.
- channel-streaming-modes:previewRecipesFile: Named controlled recipes: short-initial-preview starts with a text segment below the native initial-preview threshold, captures its pending/debounced state, then crosses that threshold and records materialization; long-final-reuses-preview captures preview before final text above the native text limit and records first-chunk reuse; final-edit-fails captures preview before arming failure on its exact terminal edit; quote-reply-skips-preview supplies a native final explicit reply/selected quote referencing an actually captured source message, not merely an inbound reply-to; delayed-finalization captures native preview, holds the owned finalization boundary, then releases/joins; hook-blocks-preview supplies substantive text carrying the existing reply-modifying hook marker while the native hook is registered, recording hook invocation and preview decision; reasoning-stream needs actual provider reasoning deltas followed by visible final text under the existing native reasoning stream setting. The installed final-text/delay forms do not provide arbitrary midstream gating or reasoning output; these capabilities remain unavailable until supplied by an existing supported producer. A response delayed before its first byte does not demonstrate a pending partial preview. All holds/faults and scoped provider/config state are restored in finally within current bounds.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:questionMarker: Identifier-safe selector for questionRecipesFile on the actual admitted LINE or WhatsApp route; not natural-language instruction.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:questionRecipesFile: File-backed actual ask_user recipe: one question id choice, header Choice, question Which fixture option?, options First (Recommended)/Second with descriptions, timeoutSeconds 30, no authored Other option. Require an actual nonempty primary source session (not subagent or ACP worker), advertised ask_user and permitted creator; retain its existing account and route. Capture real run/tool-call id, question.list/get pending id, prompt payload and delivered platform message identity. In the LINE group, retain the actual text fallback: capture the delivered question text and its numbered options, then have the same authorized creator send an ordinary native LINE text message containing 1 for the captured first option on that same account/group/session before the question expires. Use the real message source userId/groupId and a new native message id; no postback is created or required for this group question. The supplied group must already admit this plain answer under its actual mention and sender policy; do not widen policy or forge an activation/authorization field. Let native text-answer handling resolve the pending question and record its terminal state plus the original tool/turn completion; do not call question.resolve directly as the answer producer. In WhatsApp retain the actual numbered-reaction binding and captured native prompt message key, or record only the explicitly exercised plain-text fallback. Never claim a button or reaction path from RPC resolution alone. answer-question-choice uses the captured Telegram option callback and joins. question-answered-or-expired runs two owned questions sequentially: resolve the first through native Telegram controls; allow the second to reach its actual 30-second timeout and record expired/no_answer. Existing direct ask_user siblings keep their own captured handles. On failure/cancellation, stop prompt publication, cancel only the exact still-pending owned id via authorized question.resolve {id,cancel:true}, handle a concurrent terminal answer by reading its real terminal state, and join native tool/turn plus control cleanup. Do not leave a prompt waiting, forge an answer, reuse a stale prompt, alter tool policy or require a user answer beyond the existing command bound. Live LINE/WhatsApp stay outside reach without already supplied real accounts and the authorized creator's native answer interaction. LINE group output has no native question controls to remove; record the actual text prompt, terminal question result and native cleanup rather than claiming a button lifecycle.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:lateNarrationScript: Use the same actual preamble/work-tool/draft prerequisites and valid configured utility model; hold the actual narration request, complete the original primary turn, then release/cancel the utility result within its unchanged10000ms native request lifetime. Record real primary terminal state, utility cancellation/late result and native draft cleanup; keep primary, utility and ambient scopes separate.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:messageToolSendScript: File-backed counterpart of existing messageSendMarker: call the actually offered native message tool action send from the real originating Telegram route with fixture text and its captured account/conversation context, then silent final. Capture actual tool-call id, native result/receipt and source-visible output. No direct adapter call or prose prompt substitutes; preserve native tool gating.
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:tablesMarker: Select one exact short final-text response containing the valid two-column table `| Item | State |\n| --- | --- |\n| Alpha | Ready |\n| Beta | Waiting |`, where the displayed \n denotes real line breaks in fixture content. It has a header, separator and two data rows; no tool, media, explanatory prose or extra content. The same literal table is supplied to every retained Telegram code/bullets/block/off/fallback case under its actual account/peer/provider scope. Capture native HTML/plain-text or rich-message blocks after normal dispatch; the helper neither renders nor rewrites the table. A missing or incorrect native conversion after valid fixture/configuration is output, not a fixture prerequisite failure.
- mock-provider-long-reply:tableMarker: Use valid header/separator and at least five rows with sufficiently long cell content to cross the selected stream/chunk threshold; record native table-preserving split behavior. If observing a midstream interval, use the declared controlled producer or mark that interval unavailable.
- plugin-message-hooks:rewriteMarker: When consumed as inbound text, the scoped provider emits the exact rewriteMarker in its final output payload, allowing the existing hook to observe/rewrite it. Record original fixture payload, hook invocation and native rewritten output. Its mere presence in the user prompt is insufficient.
- channel-broadcast-group:passMarker: Every actually selected participant emits the native silent token NO_REPLY with no media; capture native all-pass termination. Ordinary nonempty default final text is not the all-pass recipe.
- plugin-message-hooks:deferMarker: When consumed as inbound text, explicitly select a final-text provider recipe carrying deferMarker in the actual assistant output seen by the existing before_agent_finalize/reply hook. Any native revision requested by the hook is served by a correlated next provider response, not an unrelated input; record hook action and native finalization. Presence only in the user prompt does not prove the hook-triggering payload.

Literal named fixtures:
- native-outbound-data: send-text/send-topic-thread/send-default-account/env-token-non-default use explicit nonempty native message text; send-force-document uses a real supplied fixture file and actual force-document send; send-photo-album uses two distinct supplied image files in one actual native album send. Native send-by-uncached-name and send fault cases carry explicit nonempty text before exercising their existing scoped fault. These require no model script.
- controls: send-presentation-buttons/inline-buttons-off/choose-select-option/press-command-button/press-callback-button/disabled-control/reusable-control/mini-app-button-group/controls-over-limits/optional-pin-fails/required-pin-fails/pin-owner-closes construct actual native presentation controls on a fresh owned message, capture their generated identity and creator binding before interaction. Use the native CLI/RPC payload route already named by the phase. For over-limits supply actual controls exceeding the inspected native bound, not a prompt asking for many; for pin-chunked-message use existing longWord and capture actual chunks/pin target. No stale persona control ids or invented platform result. Keep existing native permission/fault setup and cleanup.
- incoming-context: rolling-history/long-paste/platform-history-window/inbound-location and ordinary admission/name/binding cases construct literal incoming events and prior fixture history. They do not depend on a specific model output; no new response script is required unless the observation additionally needs output shape.

- channel-broadcast-digest-six: a separate pre-supplied conformance account/group with six distinct normalized agents already present in the native roster, a qualified broadcast entry with maxRounds at least 2 and maxTurns at least 12, no exclusive ACP route, an admitted sender and ordinary allowed delivery. The account/group and provider capacity are separate from the two-participant room and ambient work. Keys accountId, groupId, senderPeer, agent1, agent2, agent3, agent4, agent5, agent6, marker and script contain simple identifiers or a recipe path; each agent key supplies a native matching mention token without spaces. The helper verifies this supplied configuration and creates no agent, account, route or configuration.
The six-participant script scopes responses to the actual six actors. Each round-one final contains exactly 5000 distinguishable non-silent ASCII fixture characters; actual settled native finals and round-two continuations are captured. Each continuation excludes its own final, leaving five sibling finals truncated individually to 4000 before the joined sibling body is capped at 16000. Record lengths, hashes and owned boundary-marker positions for that body separately from the prefixed round instruction, without exporting complete provider conversations. Each round-two response returns NO_REPLY and owned work settles within 180000 milliseconds. Missing actors, continuation, delivery, isolation or native turn budget is unavailable before crediting this fixture. This separate case supplies aggregate-overflow coverage for condition 135; the two-participant case alone supplies only per-final truncation.

Subagent completion fixture content:
- mock-provider-tool-calls:parentCompletionMarker: Identifier-safe trigger selecting parentCompletionScript for an actual direct channel parent.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:parentCompletionScript: Scoped parent/child/resumption script with the exact native lifecycle below; no response is selected by task prose alone.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:toolOnlyParentCompletionMarker: Identifier-safe trigger selecting toolOnlyParentCompletionScript for the real admitted tool-only group parent.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:toolOnlyParentCompletionScript: Same native parent/child/yield lifecycle, with a host-authored waiting acknowledgment and resumed-parent message.send under the group's actual policy.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressYieldScript: Retain its existing native progress_card work and capture distinct parent/child identities. Spawn a hidden native run with completionTarget parent and cleanup delete, observe actual child request, consume the native accepted spawn result, call sessions_yield and capture yielded before child release. Capture real draft writes while yielded, release child result, observe the native resumed-parent request and its actual channel final/message delivery, then join child, parent and native cleanup. Apply the common recipe cancellation bounds; no fabricated completion event.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:restrictedDelegateScript: Retain the existing source-policy and queued-followup experiment. Use exact native spawn childSessionKey/runId and child execution; if it selects parent completion, consume the spawn result and use the real yield/resumption cycle with child join. A spawn/child tool denial remains the measured restriction, with unavailable downstream intervals instead of invented activity. Do not credit this policy recipe as a delivered subagent completion unless a correlated native channel receipt was actually captured.
The parent-completion recipe captures a real admitted channel parent, sessions_spawn accepted childSessionKey/runId, actual child execution and the consumed spawn result before the parent calls sessions_yield. A separately owned child response is held only through the captured yielded state, then released; its native terminal event resumes the same parent. The resumed parent uses the actual allowed channel delivery path, including message.send plus NO_REPLY under tool-only policy, and records a native receipt before cleanup. Native waiting acknowledgement and delivered completion are distinct observations. Child runTimeoutSeconds 30 and the existing 60000-millisecond wrapper deadline bound the flow. Cleanup targets only captured child run IDs through native subagents cancel and joins them; no completion metadata is fabricated.

The cooperative suspension observation is one bounded gateway-suspension invocation. It first records native draining while the owned ambient turn is held, then uses the same controller's scoped park/join operation and captures ready for the exact returned suspension token. It records the prepared state, resumes that token before its native lease expires, and only after native running reopens admission does it unpark and capture ambient work again. No ordinary active guard runs inside the deliberately parked/closed interval. Expiry, conflicting ownership or an unresolved blocker is recorded and cannot substitute for ready or explicit resume. The thaw helpers retain their separate park-before-idle-preflight sequence.

- channel-streaming-modes:reasoningMarker: selects the actual reasoning-stream producer in previewRecipesFile on the explicitly selected reasoning account/route. It must emit actual supported provider reasoning deltas, then final text, and capture native onReasoningStream/draft lifecycle; reasoningDefault stream or a held final alone is insufficient. Unsupported producer capability remains unavailable.
- mock-provider-markdown-reply:imageMarker: selects reply-markdown-image in renderRecipesFile on the supplied Telegram Markdown route, using the declared actual served fixture media URL and literal Markdown image output. Plain user text does not establish image output.
The live removal fixture supplies a matching existing fixture-owned bot reaction for every captured removal target. WhatsApp uses the same complete captured group key in both the removal and replacement cases: selected account, whatsappGroupJid, whatsappMessageId, participant and fromMe true. Replacement establishes its baseline with its first native add and clears its own final test reaction; it does not depend on a preceding removal phase being selected. These remain live-account observations outside reach.

Captured preview failure:
- channel-streaming-modes:previewRecipesFile: The final-edit-fails recipe additionally implements the captured noneditable-target sequence described by telegram-shim-scenario --edit-error message-too-old: real visible partial first, no intervening partial update, then a different terminal final and a one-shot captured edit rejection. Plain held-turn, initial provider delay and unchanged final text are invalid substitutes. The producer and terminal-boundary observation remain unavailable until supported; no synthetic edit or queue/session mutation.
- channel-telegram-shim:streamingScenarioAccountId: For the final-edit-fails recipe, the dedicated shim account/chat supplies ordinary sendMessage plus editMessageText preview support. Capture real numeric preview identity and restore all scoped state; the error response is a fixture-owned 400 noneditable rejection, not an assertion that a fresh message naturally aged.
Conditions 364 and 394 share the retained final-edit-fails observation, including the exact modeled Telegram HTTP400 noneditable-target rejection. The removed old-draft flow supplied no prior preview and is not counted as a separate variant.

CLI lifecycle fixture:
- channel-cli-teardown:fixtureFile: Nonsecret descriptor for an already installed task-owned local channel fixture and child-only callback modes/side-channel paths.
- channel-cli-teardown: Plugin register uses api.registerChannel for the selected channel, api.on gateway_stop for condition 284, and managed api.lifecycle.onDispose for condition 285. All gates are finite and callbacks log only fixture nonce/PID/timing/phase, not secrets or unrelated state.
- channel-cli-teardown: The selected native CLI runtime must load this local plugin during message send --dry-run. Capture actual registration identity and relevant callback entry in every fresh child; the Gateway process must not receive these fixture modes.
- channel-cli-teardown: Three stop-hook subprocess modes: slow3500ms success; prompt throwing hook success; prompt throwing hook plus fixture target-normalization failure. Disposal mode: onDispose enters and remains genuinely pending for up to8000ms, crossing the actual5s reporting grace; release after native deferral capture or finite cap and join.

channels-test-accounts additionally supplies startupStatusFixtureFile, a nonsecret definition of an already configured task-owned conformance plugin service registered through public api.registerService and its private one-shot gate storage. That service's async start callback remains pending until an external matching release or a hard sixty-second timer, with stop cleanup and no effect when unarmed. The fixture records actual callback/PID identity and keeps its gate available outside startup-gated Gateway RPC. It uses the same disposable Gateway, local config/state paths and existing service supervisor as the real channels status command. This gate is missing from the current conformance plugin; a marker file by itself is not native pending-start evidence.
Condition 247 is the channels status CLI result returned when its local Gateway startup wait reaches the native command deadline while /startupz still reports HTTP503 with status starting and pendingReason. event-status-during-startup now captures that interval through a declared public plugin-service start promise, observes the real CLI while the promise remains pending, then releases and joins startup before restoring ambient work. A channel account's lifecycle value, a completed restart, a blocked gateway_start observer or the separate database-inspection flow does not substitute for this CLI result. The helper and service gate remain unavailable until supplied; no execution is claimed.

Native preview and output recipes:
- channel-streaming-modes:previewRecipesFile: Contains the idempotent-edit-retried, preview-cleanup-fails, progress-long-final and related existing cases specified by their owning support.toWrite helper contracts. Recipe metadata must supply the native producer, scoped capture/fault and cleanup inputs those contracts require; it does not add provider protocol fields or bypass native eligibility. live-block-output supplies actual bounded text/block production through the already supported provider on the exact live account: two distinguishable content segments crossing the native configured block boundary before terminal completion, with captured native block/message identities or absence. Do not mistake multiple chunks emitted only after completion for a preceding preview. Missing external adapter/provider capability remains unavailable; live account requirements and original 40/60-second bounds remain. This selector does not apply to ordinary Mattermost onchar/routing cases. For Teams, block mode enables native completed-block delivery and flushes accepted blocks progressively; it does not promise personal-chat native partial preview on a shared channel. Emit actual supported completed reply blocks and observe their delivery before final completion, preserving the current Teams root configuration. QQ Bot is external and absent from the pinned source tree: its documented default block behavior remains a live-account/plugin prerequisite, with no claim of implemented fixture support.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:approvalPromptScript: Only when explicitly selected with --recipe approval-after-progress, first execute and capture meaningful native progress_card/tool output for that same session before issuing the already declared real exec approval request. Preserve original native prompt identity, authorized resolution/cancellation and bounded join. This branch does not replace ordinary approval-only uses.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressScript: When selected by message-agent-broadcast-room, scope the recipe independently to every participant actually admitted by the native broadcast owner and capture its real tool/progress activity, labels and native draft identity or absence before terminal completion. Retain maxTurns, ordering, mention selection and per-participant agent/session/account identity; do not launch a rejected participant, share a positional provider counter, print fake progress or require a draft as setup success. The existing 15-second case deadline includes all admitted participants and cleanup; no extension or tool-policy widening. Qualified broadcast is the existing negative-preview condition 129: do not enable or require drafts. Its native group context remains intact while genuine progress output is attempted; record suppression or unexpected preview as product output.
- mock-provider-tool-calls:progressMarker: For an existing phase observing preview/progress mode, positive production uses actual native plan/tool activity with bounded scoped producer holds that permit the native preview stage to occur before final completion. Record actual callback/message identity or absence; tool-shaped prose and elapsed delay alone are not draft evidence. Preserve off/block/no-edit/disabled modes as negative variants, never enable a draft to obtain output.
- mock-provider-long-reply:marker: For existing mode observations, use actual controlled text production matching the native mode boundary, capturing partial/block output before terminal completion where that mode supports it; an immediate long final only establishes final chunking. Keep off/progress/no-edit negative or mode-specific behavior native. Unsupported provider staging is an explicit missing capability; after supported production starts, native absence is an observed result.
- channel-streaming-modes:previewFinalScript: Ensure terminal text differs from the last accepted preview and that the controlled producer does not send it as an intervening accepted partial. Otherwise native finalization may reuse already-visible content without edit. Record native finalization/absence without forcing an edit.
- channel-telegram-shim:streamingScenarioAccountId: The idempotent-edit-retried and message-too-old final-edit cases explicitly save/apply/restore richMessages false together with their partial-mode settings so both start from native sendMessage and editMessageText. Rich-message rendering remains covered by its separate named cases.

Equivalent command observations share one retained flow with the union of their source selectors and persona requirements. Condition 390 uses message-agent-progress-drafts; the accepted-error branch of conditions 369 and 576 uses event-preview-retained-or-unused, while media, explicit-reply and unused-preview branches remain separate. Generic heartbeat observations share event-internal-wake-through-inbound. Static channel inventory/status and directory requests are each recorded once per real flag, channel and query variant. Static Doctor findings, including condition 644 and the retired Microsoft Teams files, use check-channel-status-broken-plugin; diagnostic reads after actual policy changes, failed connections, retry exhaustion or resubmission remain separate. Different identities, nonempty configuration patches, native timeout parameters, fault stages and before/after lifecycle observations are retained. These consolidations change command placement only; the recorded conditions, entry points, discovery totals and live-account limits are unchanged.

Observation outcomes and incomplete continuation:
After supported fixture input, captured ownership and the required native interval have been established, an unexpected success, denial, missing transition or failure is product output. Helpers retain that result and skip dependent actions whose native boundary is absent; they never fabricate the expected state. Missing initial fixture capabilities remain unavailable prerequisites. If cleanup or restored custody cannot safely permit later work, the helper preserves its observations and exits 2 for incomplete continuation, making the phase incomplete and verdict unresolved under the verification contract.

plugin-message-hooks additionally supplies broadcastMarker and broadcastFixtureFile. broadcastMarker is a message base distinct from the existing broad cancellation, rewrite and defer markers. broadcastFixtureFile identifies the existing channel-probe-conversations accountId, dmPeer as the cancellation target and secondDmPeer as the send-failure target, with their distinct native canonical identities and exclusive fixture ownership. For the wrapper's generated invocation payload, the installed public message_sending hook cancels only the first channel/account/target tuple; the second target is not cancelled or rewritten by that rule. One separate fault throws a permanent proven-no-send error only from the second target's actual public send callback. Both capabilities must run in the native CLI execution process, with scoped observations and bounded cleanup; the current generic Gateway fault method is not assumed to configure a fresh CLI registry. These fixture capabilities remain unavailable until supplied. (broadcastMarker, broadcastFixtureFile)

The message.broadcast failure flow uses one explicit conformance account and two distinct native targets. A fresh invocation payload is cancelled only for the first target through the existing public message_sending hook; one separately scoped send fault belongs only to the second target. The existing message-cli wrapper owns arming, the actual CLI child, every native target result and cleanup. It records unexpected outcomes after valid setup instead of classifying a missing intended failure as unavailable. The ordinary tool broadcast also names its channel/account explicitly, because an omitted broadcast channel enumerates configured providers. Standalone cancelMarker coverage and model-participant broadcast failures retain their separate behavior.

Live iMessage variants now select actual configured account and host fixtures. Local and SSH transport, pre-existing Automation denial and attachment-disabled media use actual native settings and inputs. The former remote poll and attachment-reply recipe is withdrawn because its positive vote changed supplied poll state shared by comparison clones; condition 486 remains uncovered. Its bridge and Gateway recovery cases require genuine task-owned dated rows and captured GUID notifications; local startup recovery uses the actual cursor/watermark and two-hour window, while the remote bridge case uses the fifteen-minute live fence. No timestamps, database rows or recovery cursors are rewritten. WhatsApp group cases supply distinct native mention, number, pattern, quote, ephemeral and view-once metadata through a real sender; non-default and self-chat cases select their actual configured accounts. The quoted-bot branch of condition 116 stays in event-implicit-quote. The added files are unavailable fixture descriptions for existing helpers, and these native host/account cases remain outside reach. Conditions 223 and 482 require their specific native receive-permission and transport observations; neither is inferred from a different permission failure or a successful send. Recorded condition and entry-point inventories and planned coverage counts are unchanged. The supplied auxiliary environments use env-whatsapp-on-demand:env and env-whatsapp-proxy:env and are cloned for each run. Conditions 202 and 203 use event-whatsapp-relogin-loop and event-whatsapp-request-timeout-loop respectively; each owns its real connection interval through native status/log/Doctor capture and only then releases controls and joins recovery. Their redundant readback phases are consolidated with the same source selectors and prerequisites.
