- env-whatsapp-on-demand: Supplied fixture content for the exact native recipes defined by channel-inbound-turn in support.toWrite; availability and exclusive ownership must be established before input. (env)
- env-whatsapp-on-demand:env: Pre-existing approved persona environment whose native WhatsApp package/account content is already supplied but whose runtime has not activated that channel.
- env-whatsapp-on-demand: The executor clones this environment separately for every run/side, like the primary persona, and resolves {persona:env-whatsapp-on-demand:env} to that run-owned clone. Native OCM positional selection is @{persona:env-whatsapp-on-demand:env}; helpers receive the resolved value through --env. Never operate on the durable source environment.
- env-whatsapp-on-demand: The fixture file verifies the actual clone identity, approved installed native package and selected account before normal login/activation. It neither provisions an environment nor installs/downloads a plugin or changes an external host, service account, credentials, permissions or trust. Missing pre-existing content is unavailable. Observe native lazy loading of already supplied content; do not claim new installation was exercised.
- env-whatsapp-proxy: Supplied fixture content for the exact native recipes defined by channel-inbound-turn in support.toWrite; availability and exclusive ownership must be established before input. (env)
- env-whatsapp-proxy:env: Pre-existing approved persona environment with a task-owned selected WhatsApp test account and an already supplied unreachable proxy process environment.
- env-whatsapp-proxy: The executor clones this environment separately for every run/side, like the primary persona, and resolves {persona:env-whatsapp-proxy:env} to that run-owned clone. Native OCM positional selection is @{persona:env-whatsapp-proxy:env}; helpers receive the resolved value through --env. Never operate on the durable source environment.
- env-whatsapp-proxy: Use only the supplied approved content and existing clone process environment. No provisioning, proxy installation, external host mutation, service-account creation, permission/trust change or credential alteration occurs here. Verify the real selected process proxy route before invoking the native CLI/RPC; never set a proxy variable during this observation.

Native send fixture prerequisites:
- channel-telegram-shim:preconnectAccountId: Already configured dedicated Telegram fixture account with its own synthetic token and exclusively supplied loopback endpoint. It can access the fixture dmChatId, is not ambient or any other scenario account, and no other account/controller uses its listener. No account creation or configuration change is performed by the observation. Its pre-supplied channels.telegram.accounts[preconnectAccountId].apiRoot explicitly names the owned loopback root from preconnectFixtureFile; the effective account override must differ from every shared shim apiRoot. Native account configuration supports this field; do not use a process-global API override.
- channel-telegram-shim:preconnectFixtureFile: Nonsecret manifest of the supplied exclusive shim listener/controller identity, bound loopback host/port, exact preconnectAccountId, controlled socket/listener close/rebind capability and native retry-diagnostic observer. Contains no credential values. Preflight verifies no other account/ambient/controller depends on this endpoint. Missing ownership/capability is unavailable; never substitute the shared Telegram shim. Capture the effective native account apiRoot and compare it to this manifest before closing the endpoint. Resolve the account through its actual merged Telegram config, not merely a persona label.
- channel-probe-conversations: The native batch cases use the existing accountId/dmPeer plus a captured real configured session/delivery context when recording route/mirror evidence; the future conformance-plugin method invokes public sendDurableMessageBatch with two or three literal source payloads. It must record real public adapter callbacks and receipts; no new model batch response grammar, private product hook, store mutation or reconciliation proof is supplied.
- plugin-message-hooks: For durable-batch.mixed-outcomes, cancellation targets only source payload B through its exact marker, account, target and invocation. A and C do not carry the cancellation marker; C's provider failure is separate and must reach its actual callback. Restore only this fixture predicate after the batch.

Ordinary conformance Gateway send and message.action observations each have one canonical invocation; changing only a short payload label or fresh idempotency token does not create another variant. The receipt event retains one CLI dry-run with its failed/suppressed reply sequence and route-state observations. Selectors and prerequisites from removed duplicates are carried to those owners. Queued sends, hook markers, formatting/length/media, explicit source contexts, native entry points and captured resource or same-key retry sequences remain distinct. No condition, entry point or recorded discovery share changes.

Native recovery fixture qualification:
- channel-telegram-shim:preconnectFixtureFile: Durable recovery/exhaustion also uses this same pre-supplied exclusive endpoint. Its controller must keep the listener live for inbound admission/provider hold, then establish actual refused connect across every native send retry and retain the outage across any Gateway restart. It records safe close/rebind and real fault consumption, contains no product queue data, and restores the endpoint before account reactivation. A failure branch with socket reset, post-send evidence or missing syscall proof remains the native uncertain outcome.
- channel-probe-conversations: Recovery no-send variants require the future existing fixture's actual public message adapter callback to throw retryable PlatformMessageNotDispatchedError before any visible effect. Its external fault controller is scope-bound across restart; native queue identity/owner/state comes only from real core persistence. Confirmed-owner observation uses the public afterSendSuccess callback only after a real receipt. Neither capability changes product queue data or supplies automatic unknown-send reconciliation.
- channel-delivery-queued: Do not infer separate session-delivery-queue or ingress-drain backlog from an outbound send failure. The corrected restart/recovery flows create and capture one actual outbound delivery plus any native completion owner; a separately claimed session-delivery row needs its own existing native producer and captured identity.
- channel-telegram-shim:preconnectAccountId: Recovery/exhaustion uses this actual pre-supplied native apiRoot account. The owning helper temporarily applies/restores final-only account streaming after native adoption, while its endpoint is still live; inbound admission and a real scoped held provider request precede closing the endpoint. Endpoint restoration precedes native account restoration after every observed restart/outage.
Replayable outbound recovery requires an actual retained no-send row before restart; ambiguous delivery stays separate. The existing recovery helper owns scoped fault release, endpoint reopening before account restoration, native scheduler observations and bounded cleanup. Generic failure does not establish a separate ingress or session-delivery backlog.

Remaining live settings now use actual account-level transport, identity, scope, credential-source, daemon and mode fixtures. Room-specific variants retain their actual native membership, allowlist, invite, thread and encryption facts; peer names do not configure these states. Slack Socket Mode and HTTP accounts are distinct, and real thread inputs carry both channel and thread identity. Matrix invites use native membership events and selected auto-join accounts; Signal selects its actual daemon/account; workplace and external plugin cases require their real native configuration/event contracts. Existing real-platform exclusions remain. Generic condition 558 is now planned through the existing conformance admission fixture with an explicitly present authentication map omitting a declared field, preserving native denial and without forcing authorization. Its unsupported native Slack producer is not claimed. Condition 574 retains event-ingress-deferred-slots as its finite-capacity conformance owner; the unsupported Slack-only duplicate is removed. That earlier correction added one planned condition and no new condition, entry point or derivation credit.

Matrix supplied content for channel-real-accounts and channel-matrix-native-fixtures must already have effective name and avatarUrl absent for every configured Matrix account that these environments can start, including inherited channel-root values, before persona export and before any comparison clone starts its Gateway. This includes the ordinary Matrix account, the dedicated preview account and all Matrix native-fixture account keys. The native startup profile synchronizer then skips its own-profile work without reading or writing the remote profile. Incoming membership display names, room-name policy keys and other users' profiles remain valid fixture input; they are not the bot's own name or avatarUrl settings. This is a missing supplied-content prerequisite, not permission for a helper or builder to clear live account settings, alter a remote profile, create credentials or provision a replacement account. If the existing supplied content does not satisfy it, the corresponding Matrix observations remain unavailable before clone startup. A late check after Gateway startup is insufficient. The existing persona build/check/export contract must establish this content; no new manifest key, placeholder, schema, lock or coordination API is introduced. Other live-platform custody and account-free reach limits remain unchanged.

Condition 573 remains outside reach: no supplied native ownership-transition fixture can take one exact still-live claim from another owner during fan-in adoption. A callback-injected exception or direct database identity rewrite is not that event. The false combined recipe is removed; successful native multiple-claim settlement remains planned under 572. That earlier correction subtracted one fully planned condition; inventory, entry points, document headings and recorded discovery shares stayed unchanged. The separate condition 558 observation and all later qualifications are included in the current coverage total at the start of this document.

The Mattermost registration/callback condition has one retained owner. Its actual named-account startup, real registration rejection and real callback precede cleanup; starting an already running default account or reading unrelated logs does not establish this condition. Native HTTP outcomes are recorded without forcing an unauthorized status.

- env-channel-provider-absent: Only the cloned secondary env is addressed. It has actual channels.defaults.groupPolicy open and no per-provider override; real plugin/account startup does not author a channel block. Neither preparation nor observation removes the primary provider that owns ambient work. No new environment, plugin installation, credentials or config mutation is performed here. env: Pre-existing approved environment with installed/enabled existing conformance plugin, its real default fixture account and actual absent channels.kova-channel-probe block; executor-cloned separately per run/side.

- config-provider-block-missing: The missing state is the actual source/runtime provider block absence, never a primary-clone room label. The real default is open so the observed allowlist fallback is distinguishable from a configured allowlist. Native per-process warning observations are retained from the actual account lifecycle; no cache reset. accountId: Actual default conformance fixture account in env-channel-provider-absent:env, still discoverable/running with the channel block absent.; groupId: Owned synthetic group used for two distinct normal SDK admission events in that environment.; senderPeer: Owned synthetic sender, admitted or denied through the actual resolved policy.

- env-channel-remote-auth: Use @{persona:env-channel-remote-auth:env} for native positional environment selection; helpers receive the resolved clone through --env. It contains the already installed Telegram adapter and owned saved synthetic auth metadata. Do not start a local Gateway in this client-only clone or provision/configure a remote endpoint. No durable source persona, primary Gateway or external host is changed. env: Pre-existing approved client-only environment, cloned per run/side exactly like the primary persona; actual gateway.mode remote with remote.url absent and no target overrides.

- config-gateway-remote: This declaration supports261 only. It describes pre-existing content in the separate client clone, not gateway.mode on the primary environment. Successful remote-mode auth.login and saved auth for 260 are not exercised by logout or an unsupported Telegram login call. logoutAccountId: Disjoint existing named Telegram account in env-channel-remote-auth:env with owned local saved auth and no root/default/env credential fallback.; fixtureFile: Structured description of supplied remote-mode/absent-URL/override metadata and owned local-auth artifact scope; no secret values, setup code or new credentials.

Condition 95 now uses one supplied secondary environment with the actual provider block absent, the existing conformance plugin still enabled and its real fixture account available. The public native policy resolver and normal SDK admission consume that configuration; two same-account inputs record actual warning behavior without resetting its cache. The duplicate room-only input is removed, and the primary provider and ambient work remain unchanged. Condition 261 uses a separate remote-mode client clone with no remote URL: native logout records the real Gateway-call configuration error, its exit and before/after owned local-auth metadata. This reaches the native remote-mode rethrow and does not imply that a remote server replied. Local offline logout remains a separate flow. Condition 260 remains uncovered because this logout case does not perform successful remote-mode login. The planned coverage counts and original condition/entry-point inventories are unchanged.

The inbound-helper reconciliation compares actual command options, message shape, native account/route, scoped scripts and preceding state. It removes eight repeated invocations across six groups: the Telegram same-ID pair, two baseline group inputs shared with their complete context sequence, the repeated foreign-thread reply producer, native Telegram status, standalone pin/place observations already supplied by the native location recipe, and repeated Telegram spoiler rendering. Ordered replay, history, thread-follow-up and multiple-claim producers/consumers remain. Captured SDK replay now crosses an explicit bounded native account stop/start while the Gateway process and native dedupe scope remain unchanged; immediate Telegram duplicate admission, eviction and pruned-row re-admission remain distinct. No condition, entry point, document heading, recorded discovery share or break mapping is removed.

- channel-probe-conversations: Fault-bearing reply cases require actual public outbound callbacks and already registered public message hooks, each with an invocation-owned scope and unconditional disarm. No generic fault switch supplies metadata-write failure, native queue persistence failure, claim removal or CLI socket loss. Those unsupported conditions are outside reach; do not implement them as private hooks or state edits.

- channel-ingress-monitor-fixture: Retain the native runtime-supplied queue and native monitor. Repeated admission and deferred/abandoned callbacks use the public API. The two mismatch cases intentionally supply inconsistent caller-owned codec output, a distinct input contract; no queue identity is edited. This fixture supplies no native append-write failure or stolen/dropped-claim capability.

- plugin-message-hooks: The atomic suppression/empty-transform cases temporarily activate the supplied hook-only plugin under the shared registration-lifetime owner. Their predicates remain bound to actual account, target and payload. After the native work settles, cleanup removes predicates and restores enabled:false through native plugin replacement; it awaits the disabled runtime generation and fixture disposal before later previews. No inert global modifier registrations remain between observations.

- channel-access-groups: For the declared Discord audience-failure case, the supplied or explicitly owned temporary group is native type discord.channelAudience with the actual guildId/channelId and membership canViewChannel, referenced by the selected account's real DM allowlist. It has no unrelated permissive alternate group and its synthetic sender differs from ambient/moderation subjects. Configuration is restored exactly; missing supported fixture lookup remains a pre-input prerequisite, not permission to synthesize a membership decision.

- mock-provider-markdown-reply: The existing shortTextMarker is also the explicit final-text producer for the five declared conformance --fault cases and the named Telegram required-final-send-5xx scenario. Its content stays KOVA_SHORT_OK with no tools, media, reasoning or delay. Each use has its own native provider/run/account/target correlation; the repeated text does not grant a shared fault predicate. The existing scoped provider selector and fault lifecycle must be implemented before these planned cases are executable.

Control producers now use the native message.action envelope for presentation and legacy interactive payloads, preserving their original idempotency keys and captured native control identities. Plain send RPC observations remain on the send method. Discord guild, channel, event, emoji, role, member and moderation operations select their actual fixture or live account explicitly; captured resource consumers and cleanup preserve that same account. The fixture account is the existing setup account, while live and specialized accounts keep their own configuration and native permissions. Unsupported account selection and native permission errors remain product observations. Original capabilities, condition mappings, phase/command counts and real-account reach limits are unchanged.

- channel-discord-fixture:accountId: The real fixture-owned configured Discord account. Its value is default for the existing setup, and setup, every fixture CLI/RPC/tool input, native Gateway event and resource cleanup use this same identity. It is distinct from the live Discord account. Existing specialized account keys keep their own real configurations and fixtures.

- channel-real-accounts:discord: The already supplied real configured Discord account ID, distinct from channel-discord-fixture:accountId. Its actual bot/session and guild permissions own discordGuild, discordChannel/discordChannelId, live event/emoji/sticker/member/role objects and all live captures; a raw guild ID or a generic model session never substitutes for selecting this account. No new account, credential or permission is created.

Account disabling now drives the native confirmation under the existing bounded PTY helper and retains the intended disabled state; created-account deletion remains its later separate action. Gateway offline observations use one owned OCM stop/start lifetime and include the native Gateway-owned Discord read with an explicit fixture account. No native gateway stop/restart passthrough remains. Automatic startup uses one OCM restart with a captured before/after account record, shared by the separate real-WhatsApp reader so fixture and live prerequisites remain distinct. Equivalent Telegram held-turn recovery, resumption and guarded typing observations share one captured crash and join. Ordinary send-started uncertainty and prepared ownerless saved-final recovery have separate native boundaries and inputs; the former never establishes the latter. Real lease timing, manual acknowledgement, adapter, hook-target, tool receipt, breaker, suspension and startup distinctions remain. The seven removed phases retire their writers and readers together; selectors, prerequisites and checkpoints follow the retained producers/consumers. No condition, entry point, break mapping or historical discovery credit is added or removed.

- channel-cli-wizard-answers:disableCreatedAccountAnswers: Scripted PTY answer file with one affirmative answer for the native keeps-config Disable Telegram confirmation naming exactly channel-telegram-shim.newAccount. No other prompt or credential answer is permitted.

- channel-cli-wizard-answers:disableListeningAccountAnswers: Scripted PTY answer file with one affirmative answer for the native keeps-config Disable Telegram confirmation naming exactly channel-telegram-shim.removeListeningAccount; that supplied account is actually running before invocation.

Condition 183 now supplies both native outbound location forms: coordinates alone and coordinates with both venue name and address. The incomplete-venue and mixed-text rejection inputs remain separate. Condition 163 now includes the promised explicit stop request in the configured ambient room, with its real authorized sender and native abort classification; mention, ordinary command and direct-message observations remain. These added inputs preserve the existing condition inventory and planned counts.

- channel-discord-fixture:forumChannelId: An existing task-owned native GuildForum channel on the same explicit fixture account, distinct from ordinary channelId and all existing threads. The fixture must support real forum creation and ordinary thread message requests plus scoped cleanup of newly created thread IDs; no platform account, permission or forum is provisioned by this observation.

Condition 588 uses one public durable batch with two source payloads and no caller thread. Native first-send thread creation and later payload destinations are captured together; an independent short thread reply no longer stands in for adoption. Its existing planned count is retained subject to the declared forum and SDK fixture prerequisites.

Saved-final recovery and send-started uncertainty use different native states. The send-started interruption retains condition 429 under event-send-started-uncertain-capture/event-send-started-uncertain and its own handle. Condition 633 uses event-prepared-final-recovery: a bounded genuine public inbound reply-payload hook holds before prepared custody becomes queued, while read-only native records establish replayable prepared text and zero delivery-owner rows across all namespaces. The old later network-send gate cannot supply that condition. The hook arm is one-shot, process/run-bound and revoked before restart, so recovery runs without the old hold. The unsupported ownerless-uncertainty recipe is removed; ordinary627 retains the actual Telegram accepted/lost-response, native settlement and same-route notice observation. These corrections change no condition inventory, entry points, break mappings, discovery shares or fully planned total.

- plugin-message-hooks:preparedFinalMarker: A scoped marker selecting an existing supported finalText provider recipe producing one short ordinary nonempty ASCII final, with no tool call, media, preview/block output, suppression or route change.

- plugin-message-hooks:preparedFinalGateFile: Nonsecret descriptor for the already supplied existing hook fixture: public reply_payload_sending registration without timeout override, exact process/account/conversation/session/run binding, one-shot fixture-owned arm/entry/release records, a10000ms self-release cap, owner-loss revocation, read-only native database observer, and ordinary final-text provider recipe correlation. No raw credentials or native state mutation. Missing capability remains unavailable until supplied.

QR login now has one bounded owner from actual account selection through start, current QR updates, wait and cleanup. RPCs carry the exact accountId even when provider selection omits channel. WhatsApp returns no public login ID and has no web.login.cancel; wait timeouts can retain native state, so the owner requires real terminal settlement or a later native wait after the real three-minute freshness window before scoped restoration. Forced starts can clear auth: timeout/proxy cases use initially unlinked/stopped owned accounts, while linked UI relinking requires actual phone restoration capability. Genuine accountless card controls run only in the separately cloned sole-account UI environment, without request rewriting. Pending state no longer crosses independent prepare/wait phases. QR challenges remain exclusively in memory; retained outputs/screens contain only safe presence, expiry, account and status metadata. CLI, proxy, overlap and UI logout siblings share custody, and successful logout remains logged out. Existing real-account exclusions and condition, entry-point and discovery inventories are unchanged. Auxiliary QR environments are cloned but are not assumed to be running. The existing owner establishes or reuses the same secondary Gateway with ensure-gateway-running before UI/QR RPC work and before proxy login/status observations, within the unchanged total deadline and with time reserved for native expiry/restoration. It captures and retains the owned Gateway for later same-env observations until executor disposal; this startup is not provisioning, primary-Gateway interruption or QR cancellation. The remote-logout client-only clone never receives Gateway startup.

- channel-whatsapp-qr-fixture: Actual native auth-directory/device custody is required; account label differences and copied auth directories do not establish isolation. No concurrent cases/clones share pending login or phone/session custody. Proxy account remains the supplied channel-whatsapp-native-fixtures:proxyAccountId in its proxy clone. Missing native phone restoration/scan capability stays outside reach; no provisioning or credential copying. fixtureFile: Structured nonsecret description of existing owned real phones/accounts/auth-directory scopes, per-clone/case custody, initial native state and real scan/re-link restoration capability. No secret material or setup code.; cliAccountId: Dedicated existing owned CLI-login account.; rpcForceAccountId: Dedicated initially unlinked/stopped forced-timeout account.; rpcShortAccountId: Separate existing owned nonforced short-wait account.; completionAccountId: Dedicated owned account with real supplied authorized phone scan.; overlapAccountId: Dedicated account with the already supplied actual pending-start gate and native CLI capability.; uiAccountId: Only configured/runtime/default WhatsApp account in env-whatsapp-qr-ui:env, with owned phone and actual native UI prerequisites.

- env-whatsapp-qr-ui: Native positional commands use @{persona:env-whatsapp-qr-ui:env}; helpers receive the resolved clone via --env. Never touch durable source. Actual accountless card requests and deliberate CLI-default calls affect only the captured owned account. No environment/phone/credential/plugin/permission provisioning here. Its clone need not already have a running Gateway: the existing atomic owner explicitly ensures/reuses it within the same operation deadline, captures real process identity and retains it for later same-env observations until executor disposal. This is startup of the already supplied cloned environment, not service provisioning or QR cancellation. env: Pre-existing approved persona environment with exactly one owned configured/runtime/default WhatsApp account and only that QR-capable provider. Executor clones it per run/side.

- env-msteams-empty-self:env: An already supplied approved persona environment, executor-cloned independently for every run/side, with the native Microsoft Teams package and directory contract already installed, registered and enabled, default account selection, and no pending plugin install or auto-enable rewrite. Actual source/runtime configuration and the effective CLI process environment have no Teams appId, tenantId, appPassword or corresponding SecretRef/environment values, certificate or managed-identity configuration; no usable Teams credentials are supplied. Verify presence/absence metadata only and never expose secret values or delete credentials from another environment. This is a client-only native directory lookup and requires no Gateway start, real account, login, network request, plugin installation, configuration mutation or credential preparation. Missing installed capability or absence facts is an unavailable prerequisite. Native positional selection is @{persona:env-msteams-empty-self:env}; the durable source and primary environment are untouched.

Condition 298 uses this credential-absent Microsoft Teams clone. Its real directory self implementation returns no identity when the native credential resolver has none; the distinct Discord unsupported-self case does not substitute for it. The input is supplied as persona content, while the actual command result remains an observation. Planned coverage and discovery counts are unchanged.

Outbound broadcasts now distinguish real conformance sends from the genuine all-channel dry-run. Each real CLI/tool invocation selects its conformance channel/account independently; prior commands supply no inherited dry-run or destination scope. The all-channel preview keeps native provider/account/target resolution and its resulting errors without claiming delivery. DM-plus-group broadcasts use the same owned group's actual canonical outbound target and native group classification; the current DM-only probe cannot supply that group variant until the existing declared fixture capability is provided. Ordered target identities, scoped faults, native authorization and per-target evidence are preserved. Existing conditions, entry points, discovery totals and planned coverage counts are unchanged.

- channel-probe-conversations: The account is actually configured for the conformance provider. Both DM values are native canonical accepted targets (currently within the real probe DM namespace), distinct after normalization and disjoint from the held ambient account/peer. Values do not stand for native addresses on other providers. The all-channel dry-run retains this fixture input to record native resolution/preparation outcomes only.

- channel-group-allowlisted:outboundTarget: Native canonical outbound destination and group chat-type of the existing groupId under channel-probe-conversations:accountId. Obtain from the existing installed conformance plugin's supported public messaging normalizer/resolver; native group recipient support is an explicitly unavailable prerequisite while the current fixture remains DM-only. No guessed prefix, DM masquerade, new account, permission or external plugin.

- channel-probe-admission: For already planned conformance group send variants, the existing fixture's group conversation ownership must also be represented by its real public outbound target normalization/resolution and group-kind inference. This is a fixture capability prerequisite, not a fabricated admission result or new product action. Preserve unchanged actual SDK admission and sender authorization.

The combined-condition sweep retains existing observations but qualifies six previously full rows:33 and 92 lack account-free Slack variants;75 has only the empty-turn input;304 has only capacity eviction;421 has no request-timeout producer;615 has only acknowledgement scope-off. Their named alternatives remain in the inventory and are explicitly listed as partial in the coverage record. No phase, helper, persona producer or discovery record is added or removed. The current fully planned numerator appears only in the coverage header; earlier numerical reductions are historical. Existing break mappings remain unchanged because none depends on these missing alternatives.

- channel-telegram-shim:promotedCredential: A named credential-store record for the existing distinct synthetic promoted Telegram fixture identity. It is a reference only, resolved privately by channel-account-add and supplied through the native sensitive prompt or already supplied native SecretRef choice. It is not a raw token, new credential, helper token file or environment-default substitution.

The promotion observation now has one owner for temporary single-account setup, real native guided add, pre-restoration observation and exact restoration. It snapshots the entire actual Telegram source subtree privately, preserving absence, inherited/root fields, every named account and SecretRefs. Native object merge semantics require an inverse patch with null deletions and explicit array replacement paths; a redacted snapshot or replacement object alone is insufficient. The existing setup-created accounts and their later disable/delete consumers remain intact. Only the temporary promoted account is removed by this scoped restoration. Native outcomes are recorded before cleanup and retained separately from restoration status; conflicting concurrent edits are never overwritten. No phase, condition, entry point, break mapping or recorded discovery count changes.

Configuration changes in the reload-off, mentioned-turn, message-settings, model-pin and setup/form observations belong to one bounded helper each. Every helper captures the changed native result before restoring its exact authored values, absence and affected runtime state. The mentioned-turn preparation and join are combined in event-mention-rules-change-mid-turn; its old preparation phase and cross-phase handle are removed. Dedicated account additions/removals and logout outcomes retain their existing intended lifetime. The profile-mismatch observation preserves the different profile label, records actual custom-path precedence and requires every mutation target inside the supplied disposable scope; it never assumes that a profile label prevents a write. These are planned unavailable-helper contracts, not executed results. The configWrites denial input explicitly scopes the actual origin account policy; it also owns restoration of its possible global historyLimit target if native behavior unexpectedly allows the write.

Condition 122 remains in its original inventory and document heading. Its two fresh-ID inputs did not establish a repeated native batch, and an exact-ID replay would exercise ordinary inbound identity dedupe instead of the documented combined-prompt echo. The bounded source review identified no supported fixture producer for that distinct boundary, so the mislabeled phase is removed and fully planned coverage decreases by one. Existing batch, message replay, pruned-row re-admission and native outbound-echo observations remain unchanged. No condition, entry point, break mapping or recorded discovery credit is added or removed.

The Channels setup wizard has one planned UI owner, connect-channel-account-wizard-ui, with the combined selectors and existing scoped restoration. setup-channel-account retains its native named-account add and later finish cleanup and no longer requires browser evidence. Coverage counts are unchanged.

Placed source-file callers select observations at their existing channel boundary. An originating Android app, hosted setup flow, Codex binding, operator script, workshop or talk flow is not thereby executed. Selector completeness adds no full-condition credit and does not remove existing platform/account prerequisites.

Conformance reply observations use native send with replyTo from a captured same-channel, same-account and same-destination receipt. They record reply metadata forwarding without claiming a separate conformance reply action. The distinct reply action remains an advertised iMessage entry without an executable observation after withdrawal of the combined remote poll and attachment-reply recipe; its existing outside-reach classification and discovery are unchanged. The optional conformance pin request observes native degradation when no pin callback exists; successful pin behavior remains with the existing Telegram cases. Portable-location input is sent through the native message.action params object rather than the model tool event-location string field.

- channel-probe-conversations: The two conformance reply-metadata flows use the existing accountId and canonical dmPeer plus a message identity captured from their own preceding native send. No new message-id persona key or reply adapter capability is required. The account and target are explicit for both producer and consumer and remain separate from the ambient account. Receipt provenance includes the actual producer process; a textual ID alone is not cross-process identity.

- channel-accounts-multiple:secretMissingAccount: The already supplied Telegram account is enabled and has a configured secret reference that cannot be resolved. Its native stopped/start-failure state is distinct from the disabled and suppression fixtures. The direct channels.start request records the real secret preflight result; no missing credential is supplied, replaced or exposed. If this precondition is not present, report it as unavailable rather than crediting a disabled or already-running result as secret failure.
- channel-real-accounts: Every direct message-tool action using this trait selects the actual supplied account ID explicitly, including signal, imessage and slack, and the feishu and nextcloud-talk keys. Each account owns the corresponding peer, message, file or group fixture and its native permissions. The two newly named keys identify already supplied test accounts; they do not provision accounts or credentials. Captured fresh-message send/retract sequences retain the same explicit account throughout; the former supplied-group mutation sequence is withdrawn. These live cases remain outside reach without their supplied accounts and platform capabilities.

The bot-pair burst and under-budget inputs use distinct supplied bot identities: probeBurstBotId, discordBurstBotId and discordUnderBudgetBotId in channel-bot-peer. Each resolves to a real fixture author and an unused native account/conversation/unordered-pair key for that cloned run. Ordinary bot-policy inputs retain botId. The existing helpers capture actual settings and event times, join their own work and preserve the ambient lifetime without clearing native cooldowns or changing clocks. Conditions 15 and 174 retain the same account-free reach and planned coverage.

- channel-bot-peer: Existing botId continues to serve ordinary bot-policy/context observations. The three new values are genuine supplied fixture actors with normal native envelope/account/room ownership, not generated display-name variants. The existing discordChannelId permits the selected bot actors and unmentioned messages under actual native policy. The budget observations use enabled protection and its documented 20-events/60-second window/60-second cooldown; these scoped prerequisites must not inherit a globally disabled/overridden account. Existing acceptRoom, mentionOnlyRoom, noBotsRoom, unguardedRoom and overrideRoom resolve to the intended actual conversations and distinct native pair keys for their independent policy cases. Existing Discord channelId ordinary-policy tuple and the disabled/override trait tuples are distinct from all three reserved repeat keys. No room/bot/account is provisioned or configured by this correction.
- channel-bot-peer:probeBurstBotId: Existing distinct bot actor accepted by the conformance fixture, reserved solely for the25-message conformance burst in this cloned run. Its actual native unordered pair/account/conversation tuple differs from every other bot-policy and repeat input.
- channel-bot-peer:discordBurstBotId: Existing fixture-native Discord bot author with a real fixture user identity and author.bot=true, allowed in discordChannelId under channel-discord-fixture:accountId, reserved solely for the25-message Discord burst.
- channel-bot-peer:discordUnderBudgetBotId: A different existing fixture-native Discord bot author, allowed in the same declared account/room but with an unordered pair not used by any earlier observation. Reserved solely for the five-message under-budget flow; not an alias of botId, either burst actor, the receiving bot or disabled/override actors.

- channel-identity-audit: This existing audit-on content is required by message-agent-misc-config and records actual SDK admission for its current inputs. Enabling audit does not by itself create compatible mixed-participant collection or stale evidence; condition 555 remains outside planned reach.

Condition 387 is planned through two independent native Discord turns on the existing fixture account: narration:false with a valid explicit utility model, and narration:true with the routed agent utilityModel set to the empty string. Both retain the same native preamble/work-tool activity and visible progress-draft interval as the positive filler case. Absence on a conformance channel without the Discord callback is not used as narrator evidence. The filler and late-result siblings share the same declared route, native model selection and scoped cleanup. Existing helpers and controlled-provider inputs remain unavailable until supplied; no live account is added and condition 387 retains its planned reach.

- channel-discord-fixture: The existing streamingScenarioAccountId/channelId pair supplies an ordinary native Discord progress-draft route, access and working create/edit/delete message operations. It is not a room_event or group-owned thread, and its supplied runtime does not have preview-blocking outbound modifier hooks. All four narration cases use this route and restore its exact settings. Missing route/utility/script capability is unavailable, not evidence of a disabled narrator.
- channel-discord-fixture:narrationAgentId: Already configured dedicated agent selected by the actual binding of streamingScenarioAccountId and channelId, with normal unlocked native source session and permitted existing bounded work-tool recipe. It does not share the ambient request or utility route.
- channel-discord-fixture:narrationUtilityModel: Nonempty actual configured mock-provider model reference used by native utility completion. Its existing provider can return one short status or hold/cancel its actual request for the late case; no new provider, credentials or fallback model is installed.

Native selected-history observation: event-platform-history-window uses a successful Discord guild REST history window, followed by the distinct scoped failed-fetch case. Its real native prelude puts a canonical-only sentinel into the same session; the successful bounded platform response contains two different owned markers and omits that sentinel. Evidence records membership/order in the current trigger's inbound-history content within the actual provider input, without retaining raw prompts or requiring the sentinel to disappear from earlier conversation messages. Native Discord sets recent-history mode; no fixture supplies that internal field. Telegram rolling-history remains its pending/canonical-enrichment observation and is not credited as condition 533.

- channel-discord-fixture: For platform-history-window the existing accountId/guildId/channelId/userId identify one exclusive fixture-owned guild conversation and admitted human sender, disjoint from ambient activity and moderation subjects. The declared fixture must support actual MESSAGE_CREATE Gateway frames and the normal channelMessages REST route with scoped owned history rows, monotonic native ids/timestamps, genuine native canonical-turn capture and marker-only provider-input inspection. Native historyLimit2 and no autoThread diversion are captured after scoped adoption. Missing native protocol/capture support remains explicitly unavailable; this persona does not add a live account, external service, private product hook, transcript writer or historyKind override.

- channel-group-history: Telegram rolling-history retains actual native pending/skipped input and permitted canonical enrichment; it does not supply the recent-window no-enrichment semantics assigned to Discord533.

Hook observations and native previews share one explicit fixture-registration lifetime. The supplied hook-only plugin is disabled at baseline. Only the marked hook owners activate it; each restores the exact disabled source entry and waits for native replacement/disposal before returning. Crash/recovery owners retain registration through their actual recovered hook delivery and then retire it. The Discord narration and Telegram preview guards remain unchanged, and their ordinary inputs run after real registration removal, not predicate deactivation. Existing live-account limitations, partial conditions and coverage counts are unchanged.

channel-safe-state:
- manifest: Nonsecret per-phase scope manifest for the existing channel-state-observe helper declaration. The compiled case registry alone selects projection profiles. The manifest supplies only exact task-owned account/sender/profile/agent/session/event/delivery/job/binding identities and approved native capture fields under the current run; it cannot name or override profiles. It contains no SQL, arbitrary paths, credentials, pairing codes, claim tokens, message bodies or hidden configuration. Unknown dynamic identities must be captured by the original native owner; they are never guessed from a room label or another phase.
- Whole shared-state and agent databases are not channel evidence artifacts. All state observations use the closed structural/status allowlist before output. The same projection applies inside native lifecycle helpers at existing pre-input, held and post-cleanup boundaries. Scope/schema absence is unavailable; row absence within a proven scope remains an observation. Native checkpoints retain responses/logs/timing and applicable screen/Doctor evidence; no raw state file is copied.
- Keep correlation, lifetime, retry, routing and cleanup distinctions through timestamps, counts, closed enums, approved native IDs and private-owner equality predicates. Text/JSON/BLOB bodies, secrets, tokens, opaque grant material and free-form errors never enter retained state evidence. Generic secret-name redaction and whole-row hashing are not acceptable replacements.
- ownerScopes.ingressAgedRetry: fixed nonsecret external-cohort slot naming the environment role, native channel/account/queue and eventId for ingress-aged-retry. The helper receives this manifest explicitly via --scope-file before any input or state capture; the value must match channel-ingress-aged-pending:eventId. No implicit phase/global lookup is permitted.


Nonsecret state projection catalogue:

These are closed allowed fields, not arbitrary query arguments. Every string still requires a compiled enum or exact task-owned identifier check before output; unknown values are reported as unsupported projection. JSON paths below are fixed implementation expressions returning only typed scalars or comparisons inside SQLite. No container is returned for later redaction.

**pairing**

- `channel_pairing_requests`: `channel_key`, `account_id`, `request_id`, `created_at`, `last_seen_at`.
- `channel_pairing_allow_entries`: `channel_key`, `account_id`, `entry`, `sort_order`, `updated_at`.
- Additional allowed comparisons: request exists/count within the explicit case cohort; code present as boolean only; same request / created_at / changed last_seen_at comparisons; allow-entry membership against explicitly owned sender IDs.
- Scope: Exact channel/account and manifest-owned request/sender cohort. Retention reads occur before any native pruning/list operation; later fresh requests come only from that case producer.
- Excluded: code, meta_json, all unrelated request/allow rows.

**identity-links**

- `user_profile_identities`: `provider`, `profile_id`, `created_at`.
- `user_profiles`: `id`, `role`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.
- Additional allowed comparisons: link exists for provider=channel.identity and the exact parameterized subject tuple; linked profile equals the declared task-owned profile; authorization binding present (boolean only).
- Scope: Only exact supplied channel/account/sender tuples and profile IDs; use subject solely as a bound WHERE value, never emit its arbitrary stored representation.
- Excluded: canonical_login, authorization_id, authorization_basis_json, display_name, avatar, user_profile_emails.

**join-dedupe**

- `plugin_state_entries`: `plugin_id`, `namespace`, `entry_key`, `created_at`, `expires_at`.
- Fixed projections from `value_json`: $.seenAt as bounded timestamp; $.key equals the exact known join key as boolean.
- Scope: Only the native channel-join-intro namespace and exact key derived by the pinned codecs from task-owned channel/account/conversation; never scan another plugin namespace.
- Excluded: value_json as a value, arbitrary plugin state.

**replay-dedupe**

- `plugin_state_entries`: `plugin_id`, `namespace`, `entry_key`, `created_at`, `expires_at`.
- Fixed projections from `value_json`: $.seenAt as bounded timestamp; $.key equals captured native logical-message key as boolean.
- Scope: Only task-owned Telegram message-dispatch dedupe keys captured by the real producer and their pinned native namespace codec.
- Excluded: value_json as a value, unrelated replay keys.

**session-topology**

- `session_nodes`: `session_key`, `current_session_id`, `snapshot_revision`, `entry_valid`, `updated_at`, `status`, `created_at`, `created_via`, `created_actor_type`, `owner_actor_type`, `owner_assigned_at`, `parent_session_key`, `spawned_by`, `fork_source_session_key`, `fork_source_session_id`, `archived_at`, `last_activity_at`.
- `session_windows`: `session_id`, `session_key`, `previous_session_id`, `reason`, `session_scope`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `transcript_updated_at`, `session_entry_provenance`, `acp_owned`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `status`, `chat_type`, `channel`, `account_id`, `primary_conversation_id`, `parent_session_key`, `spawned_by`.
- `session_conversations`: `session_id`, `conversation_id`, `role`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`.
- Fixed projections from `session_nodes.entry_json`: $.delivery.kind as the closed none/internal/external enum; $.delivery.context.channel, $.delivery.context.to, $.delivery.context.accountId, $.delivery.context.threadId only as exact task-route membership/equality when kind is external; $.abortedLastRun boolean; $.activeWriterRunId, $.lifecycleRunId, $.lastRunId only as captured task run references.
- Additional allowed comparisons: exact requested session row exists or absent; task route/session/conversation references and parent-child equality; display_name and label length plus equality to explicit nonsecret fixture labels, without returning their text.
- Scope: Resolve actual captured routed agent/database, not hardcoded main; exact task session keys and captured incarnations. No all-agent or all-session scan.
- Excluded: entry_json as a value, label/display_name text, route_context_json, session_entry_snapshots, transcript/event/navigation bodies or compressed data.

**participants**

- `session_participants`: `session_key`, `identity_namespace`, `actor_id`, `contribution_count`, `first_prompted_at`, `last_prompted_at`.
- Scope: Only selected session keys and manifest-owned participant IDs; serialize participant role aliases, not arbitrary identities.
- Excluded: unrelated participants, profile display data.

**delegation**

- `subagent_runs`: `run_id`, `child_session_key`, `controller_session_key`, `requester_session_key`, `created_at`.
- Fixed projections from `session_nodes.entry_json`: $.spawnDepth nonnegative integer; $.subagentRole closed enum; $.subagentControlScope closed enum; $.inheritedToolPolicyVersion integer; $.inheritedToolPolicySource closed enum; membership of each declared nonsecret tool name in $.inheritedToolAllow / $.inheritedToolDeny as booleans.
- Scope: Only captured parent/child run and session identities; per-tool predicates use the case manifest approved tool names.
- Excluded: payload_json, arbitrary inherited tool arrays, controller/requester filesystem paths.

**ingress**

- `channel_ingress_events`: `queue_name`, `event_id`, `channel_id`, `account_id`, `status`, `lane_key`, `received_at`, `updated_at`, `claimed_at`, `attempts`, `last_attempt_at`, `failed_at`, `completed_at`.
- Additional allowed comparisons: payload/metadata/completed-metadata presence, type and supported-schema availability only; claim token/owner present booleans; same captured claim / changed claim / claim absent and actual custody transition booleans or owner-local opaque references; failure reason mapped to the pinned native closed reason set; unknown value becomes unsupported-projection, never free text.
- Scope: Exact task account and captured event IDs; lane values only match declared/captured task lanes. Claims remain private in the native operation owner.
- Excluded: payload_json, metadata_json, completed_metadata_json, claim_token, claim_owner, last_error, free-form failed_reason.

**outbound**

- `delivery_queue_entries`: `queue_name`, `id`, `status`, `entry_kind`, `session_key`, `channel`, `target`, `account_id`, `retry_count`, `last_attempt_at`, `recovery_state`, `platform_send_started_at`, `enqueued_at`, `updated_at`, `failed_at`.
- Fixed projections from `entry_json`: $.availableAt bounded timestamp; $.requiresProducerClaim boolean; $.attemptCount nonnegative integer; $.acknowledgedAt bounded timestamp; $.retainOnFailure boolean; $.producerClaimId and $.platformSendAttemptId presence and equality to private captured values only.
- Additional allowed comparisons: captured delivery row exists/absent; native status/recovery enum; same/changed owner and lease-expiry comparisons; no raw capability token; for prepared-final ownership checks: exact captured IDs across ALL queue_name values, returning only projected row identity/status/recovery; never narrow to a convenient namespace list.
- Scope: Exact fresh task-created delivery IDs captured by the native owner; selected account/target membership checked before output. No unrelated queue rows or message payloads.
- Excluded: entry_json as a value, last_error, payloads, bodies, media URLs, credential refs, raw producer/platform ownership tokens.

**recovery**

- `session_nodes`: `session_key`, `current_session_id`, `status`, `updated_at`, `snapshot_revision`.
- Fixed projections from `entry_json`: $.abortedLastRun boolean; $.restartRecoveryDeliveryReceiptState closed enum; $.restartRecoveryBeforeAgentReplyState closed enum; $.restartRecoverySourceIngress closed enum; $.restartRecoveryForceSafeTools boolean; $.restartRecoveryDisableMessageTool boolean; $.restartRecoverySuppressTextDelivery boolean; $.restartRecoveryDeliveryRunId / $.restartRecoveryDeliverySourceRunId / $.restartRecoveryDeliveryToolCallId as captured task references; $.pendingFinalDelivery.kind closed enum; $.pendingFinalDelivery.createdAt bounded timestamp; $.pendingFinalDelivery.intentId as captured task reference; $.pendingFinalDelivery.deliveries[*].id/state as separately projected captured-ID/enum columns; $.pendingFinalDelivery.text presence and equality to explicitly controlled nonsecret expected fixture text ONLY as boolean; $.pendingDeliveryNotice.state / $.pendingDeliveryNotice.createdAt and captured intent reference; $.mainRestartRecovery.cycleId as captured cycle reference; $.mainRestartRecovery.revision / chargedAttempts / startedAttempt as bounded integers; $.mainRestartRecovery.reservation.runId / attempt / lifecycleGeneration as captured references and bounded integer; $.mainRestartRecovery.foregroundClaims presence/count and equality predicates only, never tokens or whole maps; $.restartRecoveryRuns[*].runId/lifecycleGeneration as captured references; $.mainRestartRecovery.tombstone presence, reason-present/controlled expected-reason equality booleans, and captured successor references only.
- Scope: Only exact captured session/agent/incarnation and native recovery cycle. Bracket cross-database projections with the original held boundary and preserve its native deadlines.
- Excluded: entry_json as a value, pending final/notice text, deliveryContext JSON, request fingerprints, executionIdentity tokens, foregroundClaims tokens/maps, terminal evidence payloads/mediaUrls, recovery operator source and arbitrary tombstone reason text.

**tool-outcome**

- `message_tool_run_outcomes`: `id`, `run_id`, `session_key`, `agent_id`, `outcome`, `run_status`, `occurred_at`.
- Scope: Exact captured task tool run/session only; provider/model labels are unnecessary and omitted.
- Excluded: tool args/results, provider/model config or credentials.

**jobs**

- `cron_jobs`: `store_key`, `job_id`, `owner_agent_id`, `enabled`, `agent_id`, `payload_kind`, `updated_at`.
- `cron_run_receipts`: `receipt_id`, `store_key`, `job_id`, `agent_id`, `request_run_id`, `delivery_attempt_state`, `status`, `owner_pid`, `owner_start_time`, `started_at_ms`, `finished_at_ms`.
- `subagent_runs`: `run_id`, `child_session_key`, `controller_session_key`, `requester_session_key`, `created_at`.
- Scope: Only manifest-owned cron job IDs and actual captured child/requester run IDs.
- Excluded: job_json, state_json, name, description, error_text, payload_json, private filesystem paths.

**binding-authority**

- `acp_sessions`: `session_key`, `session_id`, `backend`, `agent`, `mode`, `state`, `last_activity_at`, `updated_at`.
- `current_conversation_bindings`: `binding_key`, `binding_id`, `target_session_key`, `channel`, `account_id`, `conversation_kind`, `parent_conversation_id`, `conversation_id`, `target_kind`, `status`, `bound_at`, `expires_at`, `updated_at`.
- Additional allowed comparisons: native runtime-options presence and equality of explicitly declared harmless fixed options only; captured command-owner availability/refusal comes from existing native callback/config observations, not reading credential or grant stores.
- Scope: Only the exact captured binding and ACP session owned by the revocation helper.
- Excluded: identity_json, runtime_options_json, record_json, metadata_json, cwd, last_error, authorization grant/token data.

**heartbeat**

- `heartbeat_outcomes`: `session_key`, `run_session_key`, `outcome`, `priority`, `occurred_at`, `context_run_id`, `context_claimed_at`, `updated_at`.
- Scope: Only the actual captured manual-heartbeat owner/run and task-owned session.
- Excluded: summary, response_reason, next_check, task_names_json, wake_reason.

Read file existence before opening. An absent or disabled native database is recorded as such without creating it. Existing table with zero scoped rows, missing schema, absent database and unavailable scope are different outcomes. Database availability cannot substitute for required positive native boundary evidence.

Internal helper operation registry:

Internal state observations use the following fixed allowed profile sets and the owner scope specified here. These are maximum permissions for already-required observations, not instructions to add database reads. The standalone --case map above is not consulted by internal helpers.

**support/verify/channel-pairing-lifecycle.mjs**

The already passed --fixture manifest supplies the fixed retention/request/sender cohort for initial-retention and the configured account/actors for other cases. Fresh request IDs come from this owner's native operations. No implicit channel-safe-state manifest lookup.

- `--case allowlist-roundtrip`, `--case callback-no-pair`, `--case cli-rpc-resolve`, `--case dismiss-rerequest`, `--case initial-retention`, `--case offline-approve`, `--case owner-refusal`, `--case pending-repeat`, `--case telegram-cli-approve`, `--case telegram-start`, `--case ui-review`, `--case unknown-sender`: pairing.

**support/verify/channel-inbound-turn.mjs**

Use only this invocation's captured native environment/route/account/session/event/delivery/run IDs. No external manifest is required for fresh owned native records; no state query runs until the exact corresponding keys exist.

- `--ensure-active`, `--event detached-completion-crash`, `--wait`: session-topology, ingress, outbound, recovery.
- `--event callback`, `--event member-online`, `--event native-command`, `--event presence`, `--event question-reply`, `--event reaction`, `default`: session-topology, ingress, outbound.
- `--event join`: join-dedupe.

**support/verify/channel-configured-turn.mjs**

Use only this invocation's captured native environment/route/account/session/event/delivery/run IDs. No external manifest is required for fresh owned native records; no state query runs until the exact corresponding keys exist.

- `--case denied-config-write`, `--case live-cli-patch`, `--case mid-turn-cli-patch`, `--case stopped-account-cli-patch`, `default`: session-topology, ingress, outbound.

**support/verify/channel-probe-observe.mjs**
