**support/verify/ingress-aged-retry.mjs**

Receive the new explicit --scope-file pointing to channel-safe-state:manifest. Read only its fixed ownerScopes.ingressAgedRetry slot: environment role, channel, account, native queue and the same eventId supplied on the command. This slot carries no profile/SQL/column selection. Refuse disagreement before native retry input or a state read.

- `default`: ingress.

**support/verify/channel-dead-letter-resubmit.mjs**

Use explicit channel/account/event-id and the runtime-resolved queue belonging to that account. Refuse ambiguous or mismatched queues; no event-id-only global search. Existing supplied failed-row fixture remains the producer.

- `--state claimed`, `--state pending`: ingress.

**support/verify/channel-debounce-overlap.mjs**

Use explicit channel/account/peer/sender, the already passed --fixture when present, and exact native event/claim IDs captured by the SDK or Telegram producer. No added manifest for normal or legacy Telegram cases.

- `--case sdk-config-change`, `--case sdk-text`, `--case telegram-text`, `--second-kind command`, `--second-kind media`: ingress, session-topology.

**support/verify/cron-isolated-run.mjs**

Use explicit --job-id plus the native configured cron store and actual captured receipt/run/session IDs. Do not search unrelated cron stores or serialize job_json/state_json.

- `default`: jobs, outbound, session-topology.

Closed projected status/error enums:

- projectionOutcome: `observed`, `not-applicable`, `database-absent`, `database-disabled`, `unavailable`, `incomplete`.
- projectionErrorCode: `scope-missing`, `scope-mismatch`, `scope-ambiguous`, `stale-capture`, `path-refused`, `database-unavailable`, `schema-unsupported`, `readonly-unavailable`, `read-busy`, `read-timeout`, `row-bound-exceeded`, `byte-bound-exceeded`, `value-type-unsupported`, `enum-value-unsupported`, `generation-changed`, `projection-failed`.
- ingressStatus: `pending`, `claimed`, `completed`, `failed`.
- ingressFailureCode: `corrupt_payload`, `corrupt_claim`, `restart-recovery-tombstone`, `session-start-conflict-retry-limit`, `retry-limit-exceeded`.
- outboundStatus: `pending`, `completed`, `failed`.
- outboundRecoveryState: `producer_claimed`, `send_attempt_started`, `unknown_after_send`, `settlement_pending`, `completed_permanent`, `completed_bounded`.
- sessionDeliveryKind: `none`, `internal`, `external`.
- sessionStatus: `running`, `done`, `failed`, `interrupted`, `killed`, `timeout`.
- pendingFinalKind: `replayable`, `transport-only`.
- pendingFinalDeliveryState: `prepared`, `queued`, `delivered`, `suppressed`, `unknown`.
- pendingNoticeState: `owed`, `unresolved`, `acknowledged`.
- toolOutcome: `tool_delivered`, `mute`.
- toolRunStatus: `completed`, `errored`, `aborted`.
- cronStatus: `running`, `ok`, `error`, `skipped`, `interrupted`, `superseded`.
- cronDeliveryAttemptState: `unknown`, `not-started`, `started`.
- heartbeatOutcome: `progress`, `done`, `blocked`, `needs_attention`.

Stored error/message/diagnostic strings are never returned. SQL CASE may emit an ingressFailureCode or outboundRecoveryState only on exact equality to a compiled literal listed here; it returns that constant, not the original cell. NULL/absence is separate. Unrecognized values return enum-value-unsupported plus a presence/type flag without text, prefix, suffix, hash or byte sample. If that unknown value is needed for the case distinction, evidence is incomplete. The manifest cannot extend these enums. Unrestricted tombstone reasons are represented only by reason-present and an optional equality predicate against an explicitly controlled nonsecret fixture literal; no reason text is emitted. Driver/SQL exceptions map to projectionErrorCode, never exception.message, stack, SQL or parameters.

- channel-real-accounts: buzz, twitch and x are the explicitly selected existing native account IDs; buzzSender, twitchSender and xSender identify their owned real sending actors. buzzPeer is a native Buzz group target, twitchPeer the native Twitch channel and xPeer the native X conversation ID. These three adapters receive actual group/channel events with a real mention through their native receiver, never fabricated direct messages or forced normalized contexts. Their group access, sender authorization and reply route must already be usable; no account or permission is provisioned here. These cases remain outside reach without real accounts.

- env-explicit-fleet-no-owner:wizardAnswers and wizardScopeFile: nonsecret inputs for the existing PTY helper in this separately cloned environment. The answer script chooses one existing agent at the real owner-selection prompt, then configures only that clone's pre-existing synthetic Telegram fixture. The scope descriptor bounds all account, binding and wizard metadata changes and requires exact restoration of the original ownerless fleet before the following message send. No System Agent, plugin installation, new credential or durable profile is introduced. The primary clone's ambient controller is not used to govern this other clone.

Source inspection adds the missing one-shot ingress-result reuse condition as 656, with no supplied observation. The original recorded ownership alternatives of 525 are restored within that same condition; its existing context-mismatch case remains, while ownership replacement is unprovided, so 525 becomes partial. Source inspection also adds five outbound target-prefix conditions 657–661. Existing explicit Discord channel: targets supply partial grammar coverage for 661, while no complete observation is supplied for any of these additions. Two built-in Telegram HTTP transport conditions 662–663 are also added without supplied connection-level observations. Two SDK lifecycle conditions 664–665 add terminal adoption after an ordinary no-handoff return and preview reset during an earlier awaited completion; neither has a supplied observation. A full outbound SDK heading audit adds conditions 666–697 without new observations. Existing composites 576,579,588 and 590 retain their recorded meanings but are partial where their declared inputs omit alternatives. The complete bot-loop document audit adds conditions 698–704, all wholly or partly outside reach, and restores the recorded threshold-preservation alternative within 179, making179 partial. The current fully planned numerator and condition totals appear in this file’s header; the earlier 515/665 state is historical. No new helper, phase, product input or runtime execution is claimed.

The retained context-mismatch recipe also requires a genuinely host-bound resolver from an already bundled or trusted-official active channel record. A local conformance callback alone does not supply that authority. Missing capability remains unavailable before input; no origin/trust metadata or permission is changed to make the case run.

The existing channel-safe-state manifest additionally supplies nativeOutputs entries only for the fixed phase IDs in this registry. Each names environment role, exact configured account, owned guild/channel/target/resource/user/session cohort and approved same-run producer capture references. It cannot supply commands, SQL, projection profiles, field names or output schemas. IDs already present in channel-real-accounts or channel-discord-fixture must match those explicit command inputs; a guild or channel alone does not authorize every contained record. Optional expectedText entries are explicitly controlled nonsecret fixture input literals, never observed message bodies or copied history. Resource creation binds only IDs actually returned by the current native operation; record missing IDs without substituting a persona ID. Preserve the distinction between fixture and live accounts. The pairing manifest additionally supplies nativeCliList exact legacy Telegram request/account/sender cohort, with no stored code or metadata. Missing inputs are unavailable support, not permission to capture unscoped output. For native sessions CLI observations, each listed sessions slot supplies exact agent ID plus native session key for every intended route; the actual matching row may then supply its current sessionId. Missing rows are observations, not scope failure. Existing owned captures may supply dynamic IDs via explicit run-root references; helpers do not invent a per-phase capture file. The thread-list slots explicitly include the existing cli-discord-thread.json and tool-discord-thread.json native IDs where those producers precede the list; no replacement ID is introduced.

Native result projection fields:

**pairing list / existing pairing CLI+RPC actions**

Allowed: channel/account and captured request/sender IDs verified against fixture; createdAt,lastSeenAt,expiresAt; owned request count, native count, omitted count; codePresent, requestPresent, allowEntryPresent, sameRequest, changedLastSeen; commandOwnerConfigured, bootstrap/approval/dismissal/notification result booleans; closed outcomes approved,dismissed,not-found,expired,refused,native-error; Emit only outcome facts present in the actual response or existing owner observation; do not infer expired versus not-found from a generic CLI failure..

Excluded: code; meta/metadata; free-form sender/account labels; raw error or notification text.

Scope: native-cli-list uses fixture.nativeCliList exact Telegram account/sender/request cohort; existing cases use their already passed fixture case and captured fresh IDs. The native unfiltered list still executes without --account and its channel-wide pruning remains observable.

**dead-letters list**

Allowed: matched channelId/accountId/id; receivedAt,updatedAt,failedAt,attempts,lastAttemptAt; owned count,native count,omitted count; payloadPresent,metadataPresent,messagePresent; reason as exact canonical ingressFailureCode or unsupported-value.

Excluded: payload; metadata; message; arbitrary reason; laneKey unless it equals a declared owned identifier.

Scope: manifest.nativeOutputs.<phase> exact channel/account/event cohort; event-inbound-retry-exhausted includes channel-ingress-aged-pending:eventId; inspect-resubmit includes eventId and legacyEventId; resubmit-failed includes telegramEventId. No channel-wide retained rows.

**dead-letters resubmit**

Allowed: explicit eventId/channelId/accountId; actual result kind resubmitted,not-found,completed,active,unrecoverable when native output supports it; active status pending or claimed; safe before/after record fields from dead-letters list; previousPresent,recordPresent.

Excluded: result.record payload/metadata/lastError; result.previous payload/metadata/message; claim.token.

Scope: explicit native action arguments; non-JSON success/error is recognized from exact native templates with exact owned event/channel/account. Do not invent a JSON result or an unprinted record.

**sessions --json**

Allowed: owned key/sessionId and resolved agent ID; updatedAt,ageMs,sessionStartedAt,lastInteractionAt; owned parentSessionKey/spawnedBy references,spawnDepth,forkedFromParent; participantCount and matched task participant/owner/createdActor identity references only; systemSent,abortedLastRun,inputTokens,outputTokens,totalTokens,totalTokensFresh,totalTokensVersion,contextTokens; model/provider equal to declared nonsecret fixture settings; labelPresent,labelLength,labelMatchesExpected; actual count,totalCount,limitApplied,hasMore,activeMinutes,allAgents and separate scoped count; status only done,failed,interrupted,killed,timeout or absence; unknown status is unsupported-value; visibility only shared/private values already accepted by the canonical session profile, or absence.

Excluded: store paths; spawnedWorkspaceDir,spawnedCwd; arbitrary label,color; raw createdActor/owner/participants objects; unrecognized free-form setting strings.

Scope: manifest.nativeOutputs.<phase> exact expected route/agent/session cohort with approved native producer captures under run-root. Registry stores each original argv including all-agents and active values; no new CLI filter.

**Discord read/search/pins/edit**

Allowed: ok/action/channel/dryRun/handledBy from fixed native literals; matched message id,channel_id,guild_id and matched author reference; timestamp,edited_timestamp,pinned,type; contentPresent,contentLength,contentMatchesExpected and optional exact approved literal; attachment/embed/component counts only; actual search total_results,search_id presence only,doing_deep_historical_index, pagination and separate matched count.

Excluded: unmatched messages; content not equal to approved fixture literal; author profiles; attachment URLs/files/proxy URLs; embed/component bodies; raw search wrapper extras.

Scope: manifest.nativeOutputs.<phase> exact account/target/guild and task-owned message IDs; edit uses the explicit original message-id and message literal. Native result absence/mismatch remains observation, never a successful precondition.

**Discord thread-list/channel-info/channel-list/role-info/event-list/event-create/emoji-list/emoji-upload/sticker-upload**

Allowed: matched existing or newly captured resource IDs and owned guild/parent/channel/creator references; native numeric type,position,color,permission bitfields,counts and boolean flags as appropriate to that fixed form; thread archived/locked and archive_timestamp; native complete,hasMore,returnedCount,query flags; event scheduled_start_time,scheduled_end_time,entity_type,status,privacy_level; name/topic/description/location presence,length,equality to exact controlled input; exact matching controlled input may be retained; emoji animated/available/managed and sticker format_type; scoped count,native count,omitted count.

Excluded: unmatched resources; raw thread members; arbitrary names/topics/descriptions/location; user/creator objects; URLs,images,icon hashes and native extra containers.

Scope: same explicit account plus manifest.nativeOutputs.<phase> owned existing resource cohort; successful mutations bind returned IDs to that exact owned invocation before projection and preserve IDs for existing consumers. No new creation/deletion or filter is performed.

**Discord member-info/voice-status/timeout/reactions**

Allowed: matched guild/user/channel/message/role IDs; joined_at,premium_since,communication_disabled_until; native mute/deaf/self_mute/self_deaf/self_stream/self_video/suppress/pending/connected/absent flags; voice reason unknown_voice_state only; other reason presence without text; reaction fixed input emoji or declared owned emoji ID, count, matched task user references and omitted user count; nickname/activity presence,length,equality to approved literal only.

Excluded: voice session_id,token,endpoint; raw user/member/presence/activity objects; avatar/banner URLs or hashes; arbitrary nicknames,usernames,tags; unmatched reaction users.

Scope: explicit user/message plus manifest.nativeOutputs.<phase> exact account/guild and task actors; reactions retains native total counts while disclosing only matched actor identities.

The pairing UI observation keeps its actual interaction and scoped rendered/action facts. Its automatic screenshot is omitted because native sender metadata can contain arbitrary values; neither raw DOM/accessibility dumps nor a whole screen is retained as substitute evidence.

Intentional removal inputs are scoped to their consumed message, relation, reaction slot, membership or account. Each new case trait contains only the existing native identifiers and ownership references in the original environment; it never causes another environment clone, changes a target, or enables a missing real account. Generic Gateway metadata remains per method. Ordinary tool and CLI/UI helpers do not share a catalogue head with their declared intentional-removal facades. Resource captures preserve their original account and source session so later cleanup reads that exact ownership rather than a shared persona trait. Missing implementation or owner metadata remains unavailable support. These representation changes add no observed condition or reach credit.

- channel-identity-link-case: The existing member profile and Telegram sender/account identity relation used for link, list and unlink. Only this relation is consumed. Other profile contents and account state remain reusable. This is an ID-only ownership view of already supplied state, not a second environment or a request to create an account/resource. It contains no env key, credentials or mutable copied config. Its values resolve in the original {env}; the original availability traits remain required. Only this case consumes the designated relation/content; no later phase may require this case trait. Keys: account, memberProfileId, memberSenderId.
  Original ownership references: {persona:gateway-multi-user-profiles:memberProfileId} → {persona:channel-identity-link-case:memberProfileId}; {persona:channel-telegram-shim:account} → {persona:channel-identity-link-case:account}; {persona:channel-telegram-shim:memberSenderId} → {persona:channel-identity-link-case:memberSenderId}.

- channel-discord-reaction-case: The existing fixture message reaction slot, native account/channel/message identifiers and source tool session used by this reaction case. Reaction add, remove and inspection refer to this same slot; the message is not deleted. This is an ID-only ownership view of already supplied state, not a second environment or a request to create an account/resource. It contains no env key, credentials or mutable copied config. Its values resolve in the original {env}; the original availability traits remain required. Only this case consumes the designated relation/content; no later phase may require this case trait. Keys: accountId, channelId, channelSessionKey, messageId.
  Original ownership references: {persona:channel-discord-fixture:accountId} → {persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:accountId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:channelId} → {persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:channelId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:messageId} → {persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:messageId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:channelSessionKey} → {persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:channelSessionKey}.

- channel-live-reaction-case: Only the existing Nextcloud Talk account, peer, message reference and source tool session used by the native unsupported-removal observation. Native remove:true is rejected before the sender call; this retained input does not consume a remote reaction. It remains outside reach without the actual account and native access prerequisites. This is an ID-only view of supplied content, with no environment key, credentials, new account or copied configuration. Keys: nextcloud-talk, nextcloud-talkMessageId, nextcloud-talkPeer, sourceSessionKey.
  Original ownership references: {persona:session-main-rolling:key} → {persona:channel-live-reaction-case:sourceSessionKey}; {persona:channel-real-accounts:nextcloud-talk} → {persona:channel-live-reaction-case:nextcloud-talk}; {persona:channel-real-accounts:nextcloud-talkPeer} → {persona:channel-live-reaction-case:nextcloud-talkPeer}; {persona:channel-real-accounts:nextcloud-talkMessageId} → {persona:channel-live-reaction-case:nextcloud-talkMessageId}.




- channel-discord-pin-case: The existing fixture message pin relation, actual account/channel/message identifiers and source tool session. Pin/list/unpin stay on the same message; the message and reusable account are not deleted. This is an ID-only ownership view of already supplied state, not a second environment or a request to create an account/resource. It contains no env key, credentials or mutable copied config. Its values resolve in the original {env}; the original availability traits remain required. Only this case consumes the designated relation/content; no later phase may require this case trait. Keys: accountId, channelId, channelSessionKey, messageId.
  Original ownership references: {persona:channel-discord-fixture:accountId} → {persona:channel-discord-pin-case:accountId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:channelId} → {persona:channel-discord-pin-case:channelId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:messageId} → {persona:channel-discord-pin-case:messageId}; {persona:channel-discord-fixture:channelSessionKey} → {persona:channel-discord-pin-case:channelSessionKey}.

- channel-discord-live-pin-case: Read-only scope for the supplied live Discord account and channel plus the existing tool source session. The remaining CLI pins and message-tool list-pins calls inspect this exact scope without changing a pin relation or existing message. These observations remain outside reach without the supplied live account. This ID-only view creates no environment, credential or resource. Keys: discord, discordChannel, sourceSessionKey.
  Original ownership references: {persona:channel-real-accounts:discord} → {persona:channel-discord-live-pin-case:discord}; {persona:channel-real-accounts:discordChannel} → {persona:channel-discord-live-pin-case:discordChannel}; {persona:session-main-rolling:key} → {persona:channel-discord-live-pin-case:sourceSessionKey}.




- channel-telegram-consumable-message: The already reserved synthetic Telegram message to delete, its native DM target and actual configured shim account in the original {env}. Resolve the three together and refuse a mismatched account/target/message; do not find a recent substitute. This ID-only trait has no environment or credential. The shared shim/account survives and remains usable by later phases; only the reserved message is consumable. Keys: account, dmChatId, deleteMessageId.
  Original ownership references: account → channel-telegram-shim:account; dmChatId → channel-telegram-shim:dmChatId; deleteMessageId → channel-telegram-shim:deleteMessageId.

- channel-pairing-retention-fixture: A nonsecret ID/ownership descriptor for only the already supplied initial retention cohort, its canonical rows/account and its designated fourth sender, plus the existing legacy/owner configuration facts required by that same case. Preserve the genuinely aged timestamps and same-account over-capacity input. It is a scoped view of the supplied fixture, not new state, a rewritten database or a new environment. No other phase consumes this retention descriptor. Later pairing cases still create and capture their own requests through native admission. Keys: manifest.
  Original ownership references: manifest → initial-retention portion of channel-pairing-fixtures:manifest.

Fixed native-output registry:

Each row is an allowlisted native operation, not a caller-supplied command string. The wrappers preserve these native arguments and their selected environment; the scope supplies only matching owned identities. Repeated identical rows cover each original call at its original position. Native sessions mode uses the exact argv shown here. All other modes derive the same native argv from their explicit typed flags. No arbitrary native command or field selection is accepted.

| Phase | Native argv | Scope source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `bind-conversation` | Gateway `sessions.list` with `{"limit":20}` | Actual child in `{run}/bound-discord-session.json` via explicit `--session-from` and `--run-root` |
| `bind-conversation-capture` | Native `sessions.list` used by existing `--capture-binding` | Accepted spawn child plus correlated native fixture-created thread, held by its producer |
| `message-agent-telegram-conformance` | `sessions --json --active 5` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.message-agent-telegram-conformance` |
| `message-agent-group-threads` | `sessions --json --active 10` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.message-agent-group-threads` |
| `message-agent-group-context` | `sessions --json --active 10` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.message-agent-group-context` |
| `message-agent-dm-isolated` | `sessions --json --active 5` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.message-agent-dm-isolated` |
| `message-agent-broadcast-group` | `sessions --all-agents --json --active 10` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.message-agent-broadcast-group` |
| `message-agent-legacy-config` | `pairing list telegram --json` | `channel-pairing-fixtures:manifest.nativeCliList` |
| `route-chats-by-binding` | `sessions --all-agents --json --active 10` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.route-chats-by-binding` |
| `react-to-messages` | `message reactions --channel discord --account '{persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:accountId}' --target 'channel:{persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:channelId}' --message-id '{persona:channel-discord-reaction-case:messageId}' --json` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.react-to-messages` |
| `use-emoji-and-stickers` | `message emoji list --channel discord --account '{persona:channel-real-accounts:discord}' --guild-id '{persona:channel-real-accounts:discordGuild}' --json` | `channel-safe-state:manifest.nativeOutputs.use-emoji-and-stickers` |

The fixed internal state-projection registry also recognizes the two separated owners: channel-pairing-retention.mjs initial-retention uses the pairing profile and its explicitly supplied retention fixture; agent-tool-remove.mjs uses session-topology, outbound and tool-outcome only for actual captured native keys, with its original explicit or captured ownership. These are compiled owner aliases to the existing projection implementations, not caller-selectable helper identities or profiles. UI/PTY/logout facades preserve their existing safe status/auth-presence handling and add no database collection.

- channel-whatsapp-rpc-force-owner: accountId and fixtureFile identify only the already supplied rpcForceAccountId/auth owner and its immutable fixture descriptor in the original primary clone. The explicit force operation consumes this scoped authentication state; it does not consume another RPC account or the reusable fixture definition. Preserve the original exclusive phone/auth-directory custody. No new account, auth directory, credential or environment is created. Original channel-whatsapp-qr-fixture availability remains required.

- channel-whatsapp-ui-force-owner: accountId and fixtureFile identify the original uiAccountId/auth owner inside the same env-whatsapp-qr-ui clone. Both forced UI and provider-default observations consume that same scoped authentication state in template order. This trait has no env key and creates no alias clone. Other non-force observers retain their distinct originally supplied accounts; the final logout keeps this original UI account and its actual current authentication state. The immutable fixture definition and unrelated account state remain reusable.

Immutable lifecycle control inputs:

The following 32 cases copy only their closed nonsecret control values before the original destructive execution. The actual environment remains explicit, and all native state/ownership capture, gates, interruption and restoration remain atomic in execution. Preparation has a5000ms bound and performs no native operation; native deadlines are unchanged. Failed-event resubmission and forced authentication are excluded because they consume supplied state. The existing held input already supplies the peer for event-inbound-during-drain, so that case needs no extra preparation.

- channel-imessage-gateway-recovery: accountId and recipesFile are a scoped view of channel-imessage-native-fixtures.gatewayRecoveryAccountId and its existing recipesFile for only the native Gateway-recovery case. They identify the original exclusive local Messages account/backlog, prior OpenClaw cursor and completed-GUID cohort in the primary clone. - channel-dm-owner-pinned: A supplied native Telegram fixture for condition 35. accountId is a dedicated already enabled named account using the existing Telegram shim and an explicit configured binding to agentId. fixtureFile is nonsecret and contains accountId, agentId, the exact native mainSessionKey, ownerSenderId/ownerChatId, pairedSenderId/pairedChatId, and nonsecret provenance of that paired sender's genuine prior native approval. The two private-chat sender IDs are distinct numeric identities reserved to this case. The actual adopted global session.dmScope is main; the effective configured allowFrom for both DMs is exactly the owner identity; effective dmPolicy is pairing; there are no wildcard/access-group entries or overriding direct/thread routes. The different sender is admitted through an existing same-account pairing-store allow entry, not by adding it to configured allowFrom or bypassing native admission. The fixture main session and account are disjoint from the ambient account/session. The existing native debug file-log reader must be available from the start; this case does not modify global logging. No account, approval, binding or logging setup is performed by the observation. Missing supplied prerequisites leave this declared helper unavailable until supplied. (accountId, fixtureFile)
- Condition 35 remains the native owner-pin mismatch observation. The owner input establishes an actual observed baseline, followed by the distinct approved sender through real Telegram inbound. The native skip diagnostic establishes entry into the guard; metadata and final route/context are separately recorded outcomes. No outcome is forced and no conformance-only generic DM is credited as this branch.

The existing explicitly passed channel-safe-state manifest nativeOutputs.event-non-owner-dm-pinned-main-route must contain the same accountId, agentId and exact mainSessionKey as channel-dm-owner-pinned.fixtureFile, plus a reference to the actual scoped result under {run}/dm-owner-pin. Retain the existing canonical delivery.kind/context comparisons and add fixed presence/equality predicates within SQLite for $.delivery.route.channel, $.delivery.route.accountId, $.delivery.route.target.to, $.delivery.route.target.chatType and $.delivery.route.thread.id, plus $.delivery.origin.provider, $.delivery.origin.accountId and $.delivery.origin.to. Do not return a route/origin object, rawTo, arbitrary strings or entry_json. Apply the same fixed addition to this phase's final session-topology case; every other profile and case stays unchanged. Neither native metadata nor last-route updates promise to advance updatedAt, so use the actual stored observations without inventing a timestamp assertion. Map native unknown enum, missing scope/schema, stale captures and cleanup failure through the existing closed projection codes; never emit a raw source value or error.

- channel-telegram-shim: emojiChatId, emojiSenderId and emojiListScript supply the isolated, already authorized native Telegram conversation for the emoji-list operation. The script makes one destinationless message action with explicit accountId matching the actual inbound account; OpenClaw derives the trusted current chat and requester identity. These are declared fixture additions, not an executed observation. (emojiChatId, emojiSenderId, emojiListScript)
- Slack download-file is destinationless: its existing native tool call retains the explicit Slack accountId, owned fileId and channelId for native read authorization, with no to or target. The upload operation retains its actual destination.

The identical Telegram emoji-list observations are consolidated in list-emoji, retaining the union of their selectors and prerequisites and the original longer native timeout. The combined operation still includes the separate Discord CLI observation.

- channel-real-accounts:slack, slackChannel and slackFileId describe one already supplied file-read fixture. slack is the actual configured account used for both native channel authorization and file retrieval. slackChannel is the native channel scope in which that same account can read this owned, controlled nonsecret test file under the real invocation's configured read policy; a source-less or non-Slack model turn does not acquire current-conversation authority from the channelId argument. For an Enterprise installation the supplied scope must retain its real team-qualified target when no matching native current-workspace context exists; do not invent a team, substitute another account or provision a workspace. slackFileId is the actual Slack F-prefixed file identifier from ordinary owned Slack file/message metadata (event.files[].id), shared in that exact channel, with a currently accessible native download and size within the selected account's existing media limit. It is not a message timestamp, permalink, local path or arbitrary other-channel file. This retained case is channel-scoped: it supplies no threadId/replyTo and does not borrow a current thread. The previous upload is a separate observation and does not create this persona file ID. No account, credential, permission, file share or policy is created or widened to satisfy the tuple. Missing supplied ownership/access capability remains outside reach; actual native denial, stale/removed share, inaccessible file or size rejection after a valid invocation remains observed product output, not permission to substitute another file. All real-account exclusions remain unchanged.

The presentation audit restores recorded meanings within conditions 593–596, 600, 602, 603, 608 and 610. The second final run already described Mattermost retaining externally settled prompts and LINE notices under its condition 471, so those alternatives remain in condition 594 without another ID or discovery credit. Conditions 705–724 add distinct interaction, renderer, capability and action-resolution behavior. Conditions 595, 600, 602, 603, 608 and 610 are partial where the supplied observations omit recorded alternatives.

The ingress audit restores qualifiers within conditions 552–554, 556, 559 and 563. Conditions 725–750 add current inventory entries. The remaining additions describe distinct authentication, authority, participation and route states. Conditions 553, 554, 556 and 563 are partial; the existing batched-context observation already supplies the last-source identity required by condition 552.

The inbound consumer audit restores recorded quote, cache, media, internal-source and settlement qualifiers without changing their IDs or discovery credit. Conditions 751–771 record separate hook, media, history, callback and recorder behavior. Conditions 530, 535 and 543 are partial, while condition 536 has no supplied legacy-only producer. Existing media-only input includes an undownloadable attachment, so condition 531 retains its planned coverage. Shared group-dispatch behavior is cross-referenced to existing conditions instead of counted again.

The presentation, ingress and inbound consumer reconciliations add 67 in-scope current conditions. Typed exec/plugin approval delivery and resolution are owned by approvals and excluded by the area bound. The channel-routing audit adds conditions 772–774, including guarded recording on an existing session, competing routing tiers and broadcast defaults. The CLI audit adds conditions 775–815 and restores earlier composite meanings. Condition 803 restores logout ordering already recorded in the second run of round 1; its original credit stays there and neither final run gains credit. Conditions 29, 31, 32, 45, 239, 241, 259, 263, 269, 281 and 293 are partial because the supplied observations omit part of the recorded meaning.

The message CLI and channel-index audit adds conditions 816–847 for strict selectors, action scope and compatibility, native cleanup, delivery limits and bounded join history. The formatting audit adds conditions 848–874 for code whitespace, nested boundaries, lexical preservation, rendered sizes, default selection and formatter edits. Existing per-channel join suppression, broadcast outcome and renderer/link/default/list-prefix meanings retain their IDs and historical credit; conditions 3, 292, 406, 408, 409 and 413 are partial because supplied inputs do not cover every recorded alternative. Native block-table and code fallback observations keep condition 411 covered. Poll bounds remain provider-specific: Telegram supports up to 12 options and Discord up to 10.

The current inventory has 907 conditions: 465 ambient and 442 event. Coverage is 468 fully planned and 439 wholly or partly outside reach. The current heading inventory has 268 headings. Every original entry-point share, per-round heading denominator and historical discovery credit is unchanged. These source corrections add no derivation run or observation helper. Plugin installation mechanics and configuration writes performed by plugin installation or automatic enabling remain outside this area; selected-channel setup and credential-resolution consequences remain included.

- mock-provider-tool-calls:polledCompletionWakeFile: Supplied nonsecret fixture lifetime descriptor with fixed embedded and mcp slots. Each identifies the existing source agent and channel/account/conversation route or native MCP grant/session reference, the expected canonical source-session scope, and the existing owned-clone teardown/exclusive-interval provenance. Runtime session/process/run IDs still come from the actual source operation and must match this scope. The source owner already has heartbeat.every 0m and heartbeat.timeoutSeconds 60 before clone startup, independent bounded provider capacity, and no unaccounted prior wake/cron/system-event work before its first case. Current case-generated automatic exec notifications are allowed and observed. Reuse requires previous owned terminal settlement and restored ambient custody. The interval also excludes unrelated recurring work from other owners, including the separate short-cadence fixture. No credential, config backup, callback, raw event, queued-wake ID or arbitrary execution program is contained in this file. No agent/account/grant, timer or service is provisioned by the helper. Missing custody or an execution that retains an unsettled clone is unavailable before exec input. (polledCompletionWakeFile)

The three explicit heartbeat-wake cases and the two internal later completion wakes share the same ambient parking and settlement implementation. Standalone heartbeat owner selection is unchanged; completion wakes retain their captured source session and never borrow manualWakeAgentId. Pending exec-event completion may use the native exception while ambient is active; an ordinary later manual wake may not. Existing condition643 and the MCP completion mapping retain their meanings, with no new credit.

The embedded completion slot uses the existing channel-probe-conversations accountId/dmPeer/dmSessionKey and its actual configured source owner; the native MCP slot matches its existing client grant. That source owner must be distinct from ambient and the selected short-cadence owner. A pre-existing match with the standalone manual-wake owner is allowed only through the same real source binding and settled prior work, never through retargeting. Remove config-heartbeat-short from these two completion phases because they do not use its recurring owner; keep it globally for its real consumers. Other configured schedules still constrain the shared exclusive interval. No existing trait is silently redefined and no account/agent is provisioned.

Persistent account creation now has three separate native custody pairs: connect-channel-account-cli writes created-channel-account-cli.json for remove-channel-account (disable then delete); setup-channel-account writes created-channel-account-token.json for remove-created-channel-accounts; event-hot-reload-account writes created-channel-account-hot-reload.json for remove-hot-reload-channel-account. The files contain actual task-created account/environment provenance only, never tokens or config backups. Each captured reader selects and consumes its one matching producer, and their selectors are the union of that pair plus the actual native remove owners. No union links the token and hot-reload pairs merely because they formerly shared one cleanup phase. The existing synthetic token values and native add flags are preserved through channel-account-add's closed add form; no credential provisioning or new product schema is introduced.

The two final deletes remain adjacent at the former combined cleanup position, after every actual user and existing fixture finalization. The connect-account disable/delete flow retains its original earlier late position and confirmation. Wizard/configure UI, ownerless-fleet wizard, promotion and reload-off flows remain atomic restoration owners; they do not supply these cross-phase captures. event-remove-while-listening and logout variants intentionally consume dedicated pre-supplied accounts and are not paired with unrelated new-account producers. Collision or uncertain creation never grants removal authority. A failed creation keeps native failure and cleanup custody with the existing disposable-clone teardown; no success or silent skip is fabricated. Condition, entry-point, historical discovery and break counts are unchanged; splitting the combined cleanup adds one operation phase only.

The channel RPC reconciliation adds conditions 875–884 for omitted-account selection, explicit manual-parameter rejection, QR provider selection and opaque correlation, and the public/CLI pairing response and selector distinctions. Existing manual-start suppression meanings retain their original credit; condition 277 is partial because no supplied ambient-suppressed account exercises explicit bypass. The current WhatsApp provider returns no opaque sessionKey, so conditional forwarding language does not cover that missing case. Pairing codes and QR/session secrets remain memory-only; checking native response shape must occur before safe projection without retaining those values. No new provider, helper, account or derivation run is introduced.


Telegram completed-block observations use streaming.mode partial with block.enabled true. Native Telegram treats off as disabling all stream delivery, so setting block.enabled alone cannot enable these cases. The explicit block setting suppresses answer-preview draft rendering while retaining completed-block callbacks; final-only off/false and intentional partial/block/progress preview variants remain separate. This applies to block breaks, newline chunking, coalescing, human delay, oversized text/fences, media repetition and streamed tables. The original controller, source account/agent, restoration, reply recipes and capture ownership remain.

dm-scope-isolated supplies the exact conformance accountId selected by both inputs of message-agent-dm-isolated. Its peerId and channel-probe-conversations:dmPeer are distinct direct-chat peers admitted by that same account and routed to the same supplied agent under native session.dmScope per-peer. The control peer and channel-groups-mixed:allowedSender identify the same second person; both differ from the first person's canonical identity. Supplied identityLinks may include aliases within each person's group but must not link the two people together. The existing trait preparation/check establishes these actual account, binding, scope and identity facts before observation; no default-account fallback or post-input forced session result is accepted. The existing channel-safe-state case observes that selected account and the two actual routed identities. No new persona key or identity-link mutation is introduced by the observation.

The legacy-plugin-compatibility recipe calls the explicit channel-account-add legacy form, never the rejected default head or a Telegram-only form. The outer --setup-credential forwards channel-real-accounts:legacyPluginCredential as a reference only. Its existing recipesFile descriptor must identify the installed artifact's real legacy setup adapter and supported native sensitive wizard path, exact owner/account choices and existing clone cleanup custody. A modern setupContract takes precedence over legacy setup and does not establish this compatibility observation. Native channel help still records selected-channel compatibility flags; guided native setup reaches the real legacy adapter and the following inbound retains runtime-alias/identifier observations. No new plugin, credential, permission, schema or service is prepared. Missing safe native credential/account prompting is unavailable, and the live/external account classification stays outside reach. The 210000ms recipe and220000ms phase budgets remain unchanged.

## Bounded native CLI forms

Native CLI timeout ownership: the fixed heads below execute one original CLI command each, through the same selected OCM environment. Their --timeout is an outer millisecond cap, not a new product option. Internal status/probe, model and send deadlines do not establish a whole-process bound. Each cap includes child cleanup and approved output capture; exhaustion is recorded as incomplete/timed-out evidence. The native actual exit remains in JSON, while script exit codes keep the catalogue contract. Native command, selector, condition, fixture account and real-account reach mappings are otherwise unchanged.

The declared native forms below use one helper description per file and a distinct catalogue entry per literal head. The common required wrapper flags are --env and --timeout; both are removed before native execution. Listed flag values are scalar except the explicitly described broadcast targets. Repeated media, poll-option and sticker-id flags retain separate scalar values and order; sticker-id is not variadic. Native option names and their actual supplied values remain unchanged.

| Helper and literal head | Native command | Side | Positional operands | Accepted native flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `channel-cli.mjs channels-status` | `channels status` | read | none | `--channel <value>`, `--json`, `--probe` |
| `channel-cli.mjs channels-list` | `channels list` | read | none | `--all`, `--json` |
| `channel-cli.mjs config-get` | `config get` | read | `<path>` | `--json` |
| `channel-cli.mjs health` | `health` | read | none | `--json` |
| `channel-cli.mjs status` | `status` | read | none | `--all`, `--json` |

Config-get accepts only the recorded paths channels, channels.telegram, agents, broadcast and channels.kova-channel-probe. Agents-delete uses exactly the existing task-owned participant ID. Resolve preserves the supplied one/two entries and their order. Broadcast --targets consumes its consecutive original targets until the next known option, with at least one value; the all-channel case retains --dry-run. Repeated --sticker-id and --poll-option each consume one value, with no bare additional operand. The catalogue uses only its existing none/required/optional value enum; native repetition and variadic grammar do not add schema fields. Required/optional and variadic positional forms must be preserved when these entries are registered.

Setup's channels-status wrapper alone accepts --native-timeout-ms 10000 and forwards native --timeout 10000, inside its 30000ms total cap. Other native defaults remain; the agent's native timeout is seconds and is not replaced by a millisecond argument. All outer caps include process termination, safe projection and output. No timeout is a fixed sleep or a claim of successful native completion. A child ending does not prove its Gateway work ended; existing owned-work cleanup and uncertainty barriers remain.

For only the added fixed native CLI heads, preserve nativeExitCode, nativeSignal and nativeTimedOut in projected JSON independently from wrapper exit. A wrapper grammar/scope failure is exit2 before native input. A native parse/usage failure is also wrapper exit2, even when the native process uses another code. Native exit2 always remains wrapper exit2 in these declared forms unless a later source-backed, form-specific non-usage mapping is explicitly added; this proposal adds no such exception. Ambiguous native exit2 or insufficient evidence to distinguish a malformed call from a valid product error stays unresolved2, never1 or0. Other nonzero native exits map to wrapper1 only after the fixed invocation passed its closed grammar/scope check and the captured response establishes a valid native product/transport error rather than usage failure; otherwise use unresolved2. A successful natural native result maps to0 without asserting its contents are expected. The helper's own total deadline maps to3, retaining any captured native status separately; a completed malformed result must not be relabeled timeout. Never equate the generic native error.type cli_error with either usage or a valid product failure: that envelope covers both. Recognize usage only from the pinned, exact command/parser diagnostic forms in private bounded memory and retain only a closed reason code, not raw diagnostic text. Unknown or unsafe diagnostics remain incomplete/unresolved, with no retry, flag rewriting or raw-output fallback. This amends the earlier blanket nonzero-native-to1 wording only for the new fixed heads and leaves unrelated helper contracts unchanged.

The broadcast reconciliation adds conditions 885–895 for follow-up eligibility, pass/error budget consumption, sequential-round input separation, configured mention precedence and native delivery distinctions. Existing physical-message accounting and coordinator-scope isolation remain conditions 703 and 704; bounded final-only digest isolation remains condition 133. They are cross-referenced rather than counted again. Conditions 126, 129, 133, 139, 143 and 147 are partial where conformance-only or text-only inputs omit the recorded native platform, shared quota, media-owner or sibling-history alternatives. Existing oversize digest recipes retain condition 135. New current Fields and Troubleshooting heading mappings receive no historical discovery credit. No derivation run or broadcast observation is added.

The channel configuration Advanced settings disclosure is placed with the existing edit-channel-config-page-ui operation. Its show/hide and persisted preference observations extend the declared channels-config-edit flow; no new phase or persona fixture is added. These are planned observations until the declared UI helper exists. Original condition coverage and all real-account exclusions remain unchanged.

Condition 896 records the documented denyIn precedence when the same conversation appears in both mention-policy lists. It applies in both allow and deny mode, independently of native mention detection. The separate existing allowIn and denyIn recipes do not observe that overlap, so the condition remains uncovered. Its existing source heading receives no additional historical discovery credit. No command, persona input or derivation run is added.

## Scoped session RPC observations

The fixed native-output registry additionally covers gateway-call.mjs sessions.list in bind-conversation and agent-tool-turn.mjs --capture-binding. Both reuse the existing sessions result projection in channel-state-observe before the first checkpoint, diagnostic, artifact or capture write. The former requires --session-from and --run-root, resolving only the existing current-run bound-discord-session.json producer beneath that root without symlink/path escape. Require its actual childSessionKey, source environment, configured Discord account, routed agent and created-thread provenance to agree with the current clone and consumer; do not substitute a persona key or the newest session. Keep the actual native sessions.list request {"limit":20}; no invented sessionKeys parameter, replacement sessions.get, SQL read, retry or pagination request is added. Retain only the row whose native key exactly equals that verified childSessionKey; unrelated rows and top-level store paths/defaults are never emitted. Row absence in this bounded native page is an observation, not proof that the session does not exist. Retain native count/total/limit/hasMore only when present, separately from matched count and omitted count. Missing/stale/mismatched producer scope fails unavailable2 before RPC input. Loss of safe output after input yields incomplete2 with actual native status preserved, never raw fallback.

The capture-binding producer invokes the same internal projection on its genuine sessions.list resolution before writing bound-discord-session.json. Its scope comes directly from the accepted sessions_spawn childSessionKey/runId, source session/environment and existing selected fixture account, plus the correlated actual fixture-created thread response. Native deliveryContext/lastThreadId may be inspected only in bounded private memory to resolve that child. Verify route/account/parent/thread identity against the correlated native creation before exposing the resource references needed by existing consumers; unresolved identity is incomplete, never a fabricated conversation. No generic main/default session or unrelated list row establishes ownership.

For these two RPC forms, the existing sessions fields remain closed and the only additional retained route fields are exact matched owned channel/account/conversation/thread references, route-present and equality booleans for deliveryContext.channel/accountId/to/threadId and lastChannel/lastAccountId/lastTo/lastThreadId. Preserve the actual runtime representation of a matched string or numeric thread ID. No raw deliveryContext, origin, defaults, worktree, workspace/store path, title, label, observerDigest, pluginExtensions, goal, transcript/body, actor/profile object, arbitrary error or unmatched row is retained. The binding capture may still expose its actual childSessionKey/runId and verified created conversation/account/parent IDs for its established readers; it never retains the entire sessions result or fixture response. Existing finite memory/output bounds, closed error codes, no raw stdout/stderr fallback, native timeout and sole producer/cleanup custody apply unchanged. This is an internal projection addition for already declared unavailable helpers, not a new product method or executed observation.

The ordinary service start/restart observations explicitly bind the existing channels-test-accounts restoration descriptor and sole ambient controller. Two ordinary restarts use the established immutable-control preparation; ordinary start receives those inputs directly. Preparation adds 5000ms to each affected phase while retaining the native 900000ms recovery/restoration cap. Intended stopped and logged-out states remain intact until their explicit subsequent start; Gateway health does not substitute for channel restoration, and missing scope prevents native input.

Shared remote mutation limit: Feishu, WhatsApp and Signal removal inputs using supplied message identities have no executable variant. The WhatsApp single-reaction replacement input, Matrix voting by the supplied actor in an existing poll, Slack opening/resuming a conversation for the supplied account/user tuple, and Matrix supplied-room off/always/allowlist invitation or startup-member-update flows also have no executable variant. Their native action/condition inventory remains. Caller or broad adapter selectors may still select other observations, but do not provide these missing mutation observations. An identifier-only ownership trait and local environment clone cannot isolate a remote reaction slot, ballot, membership or open-conversation state. No new identities, services, relays, locks, restoration promises or runner behavior are introduced. Nextcloud Talk remove:true is retained because its native adapter refuses before transport. Captured same-run Mattermost/Matrix previews remain owned by their original producer and are the only messages their cleanup can delete. Fresh Slack uploads and safe reads remain; no generic live resource cleanup is inferred. Existing QR/login flows retain their explicit before-input guard forbidding shared phone/session custody across concurrent clones and requiring actual restoration capability; a missing guard prerequisite is unavailable, not permission to bypass it.

Live Discord pin/unpin, role changes, member moderation and automatic presence, plus Messages group changes and existing-message tapback removal, have no executable shared-resource recipe. Distinct case keys and local comparison clones do not duplicate remote state. The live channel-position move is also withdrawn because its native request updates guild-wide ordering. Native fixture pin/role/moderation/presence and channel-move cases remain. New Discord threads, channels, categories, uploads and events retain actual same-run created identities and their existing cleanup limits; iMessage retraction still uses only the preceding fresh send capture. Retained read-only live observations do not restore coverage to the withdrawn mutation variants. No new isolation, credentials, resource provisioning, coordination or restoration mechanism is introduced.
